Notes: Simulations use
n=100
with
n_{1}=50
under complete randomization and principal strata shares
(\pi_{\mathrm{AR}},\pi_{\mathrm{IR}},\pi_{\mathrm{NR}})=(0.9,0.05,0.05)
.
Each design is evaluated using
1{,}000
Monte Carlo repetitions and
1{,}000
random treatment assignments per repetition.
The nominal test level is
\alpha=0.05
, and the pretest level is set to
\beta=0.005
.
Rejection rates are computed using the worst-case randomization test based on the statistic
\mathcal{T}^{2}_{n}
.
The reported runtimes correspond to the median, 90th percentile, and maximum wall-clock time per dataset.

Appendix A
Proofs

This section contains the proof of Theorem
4.2
, which is included in Section
A.6
. The proof requires multiple preliminary steps:

1.

A study of asymptotic distributions of
\mathcal{T}^{0}_{n}
,
\mathcal{T}^{1}_{n}
and
\mathcal{T}^{2}_{n}
in Section
A.2
and Section
A.3
. Th key theorem is Theorem
A.12
.

2.

A study of randomization-based critical value in Section
A.4
. The key theorem is Theorem
A.25
.

Auxiliary lemmas are included in Section
A.5
.

The key technical challenge is handling the random variable
n_{A}^{1}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}
, the number of treated always-reporters. A CLT for
n_{A}^{1}
in the uniform sense does not hold under Assumption
2
; for instance, the sample may consist entirely of always-reporters. This rules out applying a combinatorial CLT to justify asymptotic validity as in
(
30
;
1
)
. Instead, we pursue a careful conditional analysis and invoke recent Berry–Esseen bounds for the combinatorial CLT developed by
(
27
)
.

A.1
Notations

Throughout the appendix, we use
Z\sim\textnormal{N}(0,1)
to denote a standard normal variables with mean zero and unit variance.

We write
\mathrm{CR}(n,n_{1}),n\geq n_{1}
for the distribution of a random vector
D=(D_{i})_{i=1}^{n}\in\{0,1\}^{n}
that selects exactly
n_{1}
of the
n
units to have
D_{i}=1
, with the remaining
n_{0}=n-n_{1}
units having
D_{i}=0
, uniformly over all such assignments.

Unless stated otherwise, all probability measures
\mathbb{P}_{n}
are taken under the complete randomization distribution of the assignment vector
D=(D_{i})_{i=1}^{n}\sim\mathrm{CR}(n,n_{1})
, with
n
and
n_{1}
understood from context.

Quantities such as the number of always-reporters
n_{A}
and the number of treated units
n_{1}
are inherently dependent on
n
. Unless noted otherwise, we suppress their notational dependence on
n
.

A.2
Asymptotic Distributions of
\mathcal{T}^{0}_{n}
,
\mathcal{T}^{1}_{n}
and
\mathcal{T}^{2}_{n}

Section
A.2.1
states a Berry-Esseen bound from
27
adapted to our setting. Section
A.2.2
states a probabilistic bound for the number of always-reporters in the treated and control group. Section
A.2.3
studies the conditional properties of the Hajek estimator, conditioning on the number of always-reporters in the treated group.

A.2.1
Berry-Esseen Bound for the Combinatorial Central Limit Theorem

Theorem
A.1
gives a Berry–Esseen bound for a two-arm completely randomized design, adapted from
27
.

Theorem A.1
.

Given potential outcomes
\{(w_{i}(1),w_{i}(0))\}_{i=1}^{n}
and a completely randomized design
\textrm{CR}(n,n_{1})
, consider the difference-in-means estimator:

\widehat{\tau}_{n}=\frac{1}{n_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}w_{i}(1)-\frac{1}{n_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(1-D_{i}\right)w_{i}(0),

for
\tau_{n}=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(w_{i}(1)-w_{i}(0)\right)
. Denote the variance of
\widehat{\tau}_{n}
as

\operatorname{V}_{n}=\operatorname{V}_{n}\left(\widehat{\tau}_{n}\right)=\frac{1}{n_{1}}v^{1}+\frac{1}{n_{0}}v^{0}-\frac{1}{n}v^{01},

where,

\displaystyle v^{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(w_{i}(1)-\overline{w}(1)\right)^{2},v^{0}=\frac{1}{n-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(w_{i}(0)-\overline{w}(0)\right)^{2},

\displaystyle v^{01}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(w_{i}(1)-w_{i}(0)-\left(\overline{w}(1)-\overline{w}(0)\right)\right)^{2},

with
\overline{w}(1)=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{i}(1)
and
\overline{w}(0)=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{i}(0)
.
Suppose we have

\operatorname{V}_{n}\left(\widehat{\tau}_{n}\right)\geq c^{-2}\left(\frac{1}{n_{1}}v^{1}+\frac{1}{n_{0}}v^{0}\right)

for some
c\geq 1
. Then there exists a universal constant
C
, which may depend on
c
, such that

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\operatorname{V}_{n}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\widehat{\tau}_{n}-\tau_{n}\right)\leq t\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(Z\leq t\right)\right|\leq C\max_{a\in\{0,1\}}\max_{i\in[n]}\frac{\left|w_{i}(a)-\overline{w}(a)\right|}{\sqrt{n_{a}v^{a}}}

Proof.

The result follows from Theorem 1-(ii) in
27
, with
F=(1,-1)
,
b=1
.
∎

It should be noted if we consider
-\widehat{\tau}_{n}
instead of
\widehat{\tau}_{n}
, the same inequality holds, i.e.,

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\operatorname{V}_{n}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\widehat{\tau}_{n}-\tau_{n}\right)\geq t\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(Z\geq t\right)\right|\leq C\max_{a\in\{0,1\}}\max_{i\in[n]}\frac{\left|w_{i}(a)-\overline{w}(a)\right|}{\sqrt{n_{a}v^{a}}}

A.2.2
Probabilistic Bounds on Always-Reporter Counts in Treatment and Control Groups

Given a vector of always-reporter indicators
A=\left(A_{i}\right)_{i=1}^{n}
, recall the definition of
\widehat{\tau}^{a}_{n}
from (
11
):

\displaystyle\widehat{\tau}^{a}_{n}(D,A)=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}-\frac{1}{n_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(1-D_{i}\right)A_{i}.

Suppose
D\sim\textrm{CR}(n,n_{1})
. The variance of
\widehat{\tau}^{a}_{n}
can be shown to be

\operatorname{V}_{n}(A)=\frac{n^{2}}{n_{1}n_{0}(n-1)}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}-\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}\right)^{2}\right).

(47)

Lemma
A.2
provides an upper-bound on the variance term.

Lemma A.2
.

\max_{\{A_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}\subset\{0,1\}^{n}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}-\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}\right)^{2}\leq\frac{1}{4}

Proof.

The maximization problem reduces to maximizing
a-a^{2}
over
a\in[0,1]
, where
a=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}
. The maximum equals
1/4
and is attained at
a=1/2
.
∎

Lemma
A.3
states that, under mild regularity conditions, the numbers of always-reporters in the treated and control groups are non-negligible with probability tending to 1.

Lemma A.3
.

Consider a vector of always–reporter indicators
A=\left(A_{i}\right)_{i=1}^{n}
, and a completely randomized designs
D=\left(D_{i}\right)_{i=1}^{n}\sim\mathrm{CR}(n,n_{1})
. Let
n_{0}=n-n_{1}
and
n_{A}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}
. Suppose the following conditions hold:

(i)

n_{1}/n\in[r,1-r]
with some
r\in(0,\frac{1}{2}]
;

(ii)

n_{A}\geq sn
for some
s\in(0,1]
.

We have

\mathbb{P}_{n}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}\leq\frac{n_{1}n_{A}}{2n}\right)\leq\frac{1-r}{rs^{2}(n-1)},

and,

\mathbb{P}_{n}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(1-D_{i}\right)A_{i}\leq\frac{n_{0}n_{A}}{2n}\right)\leq\frac{1-r}{rs^{2}(n-1)}.

Proof.

We have the following calculations

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}_{n}\left(\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}}{n_{1}}-\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(1-D_{i}\right)A_{i}}{n_{0}}\leq-\frac{n_{A}}{2n_{0}}\right)\\
=&\mathbb{P}_{n}\left(\frac{n\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}}{n_{1}n_{0}}-\frac{n_{A}}{n_{0}}\leq-\frac{n_{A}}{2n_{0}}\right)=\mathbb{P}_{n}\left(\frac{n\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}}{n_{1}n_{0}}\leq\frac{n_{A}}{2n_{0}}\right)\\
=&\mathbb{P}_{n}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}\leq\frac{n_{A}n_{1}}{2n}\right).\end{split}

We then have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}\left(\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}}{n_{1}}-\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(1-D_{i}\right)A_{i}}{n_{0}}\leq-\frac{n_{A}}{2n_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}\left(\left|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}}{n_{1}}-\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(1-D_{i}\right)A_{i}}{n_{0}}\right|\geq\frac{n_{A}}{2n_{0}}\right)\leq\frac{\operatorname{V}_{n}(A^{n})}{{n_{A}}^{2}\left(2n_{0}\right)^{-2}}

(48)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{n^{2}}{4n_{1}n_{0}(n-1)}\frac{1}{{n_{A}}^{2}\left(2n_{0}\right)^{-2}}=\frac{n^{2}n_{0}}{(n-1)n_{1}n_{A}^{2}}\leq\frac{\left(1-r\right)n^{3}}{2rs^{2}(n-1)n^{3}}

(49)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1-r}{rs^{2}(n-1)},

where
\operatorname{V}_{n}(A^{n})
is defined in (
47
), (
48
) is by the Chebyshev inequality, and (
49
) follows from Lemma
A.2
and premises
(i)
and
(ii)
. The statement for
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(1-D_{i}\right)A_{i}
can be proved analogously
∎

A.2.3
Conditional Properties of the Hajek Estimator

The following lemma says that under a completely randomized assignment of all units, the assignments for the always-reporters, conditional on exactly
k
of them being treated, are themselves completely randomized as well.

Lemma A.4
.

Given a vector of always–reporter binary indicators
A=\left(A_{i}\right)_{i=1}^{n}
, denote
\mathcal{A}=\{i\in[n]:A_{i}=1\}
and
n_{A}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}
. Suppose
(D_{i})_{i=1}^{n}\sim\textrm{CR}(n,n_{1})
, then nonnegative integer
k\leq n_{A}
,

(D_{i})_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\bigg|\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}=k\sim\textrm{CR}(n_{A},k).

Proof.

First, consider
\mathbb{P}_{n}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}=k\right)
. We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}=k\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{n}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}=k,\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}(1-A_{i})=n_{1}-k\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\binom{n_{A}}{k}\binom{n-n_{A}}{n_{1}-k}}{\binom{n}{n_{1}}},

where the first equality follows from the fact that
(D_{i})_{i=1}^{n}\sim\textrm{CR}(n,n_{1})
, and the second equality follows by inspection.

Next, consider
\mathbb{P}_{n}\left((D_{i})_{i\in\mathcal{A}},\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}=k\right)
. We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}\left((D_{i})_{i\in\mathcal{A}},\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}=k\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{n}\left((D_{i})_{i\in\mathcal{A}},\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}=k,\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}(1-A_{i})=n_{1}-k\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\binom{n-n_{A}}{n_{1}-k}}{\binom{n}{n_{1}}},

where the first equality follows from the fact that
(D_{i})_{i=1}^{n}\sim\textrm{CR}(n,n_{1})
, and the second equality follows by inspection.

The desired result follows immediately.
∎

We note that the Hajek estimator conditioning on the event that
\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}=k
, with
1\leq k\leq n_{A}-1
, can be expressed as:

\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n,k}(Y,D,A)=\frac{1}{k}\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}Y_{i}-\frac{1}{n_{A}-k}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(1-D_{i}\right)A_{i}Y_{i}.

(50)

Lemma
A.5
collects the conditional mean and variance characterizations of the Hajek estimator. They immediately follow from Lemma
A.4
and a standard calculation
12
.

Lemma A.5

(Conditional Mean and Variance)
.

Suppose we are given a vector of always-reporter indicators
A=\left(A_{i}\right)_{i=1}^{n}
, outcomes
\{\left(y_{i}(1),y_{i}(0)\right)\}_{i=1}^{n}
, and a completely randomized design
D=\left(D_{i}\right)_{i=1}^{n}\sim\textrm{CR}(n,n_{1})
. Consider the estimators
\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}
defined in (
14
) and
\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n,k}
defined in (
50
). Define
\mathcal{A}=\{i\in[n]:A_{i}=1\}
and
n_{A}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}
. Suppose
n_{A}\geq 1
and
1\leq k\leq n_{A}-1
. We have:

(i)

\textnormal{E}\left[\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}(Y,D,A)\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}=k\right]
=
\textnormal{E}\left[\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n,k}(Y,D,A)\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}=k\right]

(ii)

\operatorname{V}_{n}\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}=k\right)=k^{-1}v^{1}_{A}+\left(n_{A}-k\right)^{-1}v^{0}_{A}-n_{A}^{-1}v^{01}_{A}
,
where

\displaystyle v^{1}_{A}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{A}-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\left(y_{i}(1)-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)^{2},v^{0}_{A}=\frac{1}{n_{A}-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\left(y_{i}(0)-\overline{y}_{A}(0)\right)^{2},

(51)

\displaystyle v^{01}_{A}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{A}-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\left(y_{i}(1)-y_{i}(0)-\left(\overline{y}_{A}(1)-\overline{y}_{A}(0)\right)\right)^{2},

(52)

with
\overline{y}_{A}(1)=n_{A}^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A}}y_{i}(1)
and
\overline{y}_{A}(0)=n_{A}^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A}}y_{i}(0)
. If
n_{A}=1
, all variances and covariances are defined to be zero.

We shall write the conditional variance of the Hajek estimator as

\operatorname{V}_{n,k}\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}\right)=\operatorname{V}_{n}\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}=k\right),

(53)

viewing it as a function of
k
. In the remaining section, we let
\delta,s,r,B
be the constants fixed in the statement of Theorem
4.2
, and use the shorthand notation
\Theta^{w}_{n}=\Theta^{w}_{n}(\delta,s,r,B)
. We typically denote an element in
\Theta_{n}^{w}
by
\theta_{n}^{w}
. Lemma
A.6
collects implications of the assumptions on
\Theta_{n}^{w}
.

Lemma A.6
.

For all
n\in\mathbb{N}
and each
\theta^{w}_{n}\in\Theta^{w}_{n}
, let
\{\left(y_{i}(1),y_{i}(0)\right)\}_{i=1}^{n}
be the associated potential outcomes and
\{A_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
be the associated always-reporter indicators.
Denote
\mathcal{A}=\{i\in[n]:A_{i}=1\}
and
n_{A}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}
. Define
v_{A}^{1}
,
v_{A}^{0}
and
v_{A}^{01}
as in (
51
) and (
52
), and
\overline{y}_{A}(a)=n_{A}^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A}}y_{i}(a)
for
a\in\{0,1\}
. Let
k
be an integer such that
k\in[1,n_{A}-1]
and
n_{A}\geq 2
.

(i)

The following inequality for the conditional variance holds:

\operatorname{V}_{n,k}\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}\right)\geq\frac{(1-\delta)}{n_{A}}\left(\frac{n_{A}-k}{k}v^{1}_{A}+\frac{k}{n_{A}-k}v^{0}_{A}\right).

(54)

(ii)

We have the following inequality: for
a\in\{0,1\},

\frac{\max_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\left|y_{i}(a)-\overline{y}_{A}(a)\right|}{\sqrt{v_{A}^{a}}}\leq Bn_{A}^{1/4},

(55)

where
B
is the constant defined in Assumption
2
-
(iii)
.

Proof.

First note,

\displaystyle v^{01}_{A}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{A}-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\left(y_{i}(1)-y_{i}(0)-\left(\overline{y}_{A}(1)-\overline{y}_{A}(0)\right)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=v_{A}^{1}+v_{A}^{0}-\frac{2}{n_{A}-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\left(y_{i}(1)-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)\left(y_{i}(0)-\overline{y}_{A}(0)\right)

\displaystyle\leq v_{A}^{1}+v_{A}^{0}+2\delta\sqrt{v_{A}^{1}v_{A}^{0}}\leq v_{A}^{1}+v_{A}^{0}+\frac{\delta\left(n_{A}-k\right)}{k}v_{A}^{1}+\frac{\delta k}{n_{A}-k}v_{A}^{0},

where the first inequality is by Assumption
2
-
(ii)
and the second inequality is by the inequality
2ab\leq a^{2}+b^{2}
.

(i)
follows by the calculation,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{k}v^{1}_{A}+\frac{1}{n_{A}-k}v^{0}_{A}-\frac{1}{n_{A}}v^{01}_{A}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{k}v^{1}_{A}+\frac{1}{n_{A}-k}v^{0}_{A}-\frac{1}{n_{A}}\left(v_{A}^{1}+v_{A}^{0}+\frac{\delta\left(n_{A}-k\right)}{k}v_{A}^{1}+\frac{\delta k}{n_{A}-k}v_{A}^{0}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1-\delta}{n_{A}}\left(\frac{n_{A}-k}{k}v^{1}_{A}+\frac{k}{n_{A}-k}v^{0}_{A}\right).

(ii)
follows by the calculation, for each
a\in\{0,1\}
,

\displaystyle\max_{i\in\mathcal{A}}|y_{i}(a)-\overline{y}_{A}(a)|=\max_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\left(|y_{i}(a)-\overline{y}_{\mathcal{A}}(a)|^{4}\right)^{1/4}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(n_{A}\right)^{1/4}\left(\frac{1}{n_{A}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\left(y_{i}(a)-\overline{y}_{A}(a)\right)^{4}\right)^{1/4}\leq Bn_{A}^{1/4}\sqrt{v_{A}^{a}},

where the last inequality follows by Assumption
2
-
(iii)
.
v_{A}^{a}
are positive by Assumption
2
-
(ii)
for
a\in\{0,1\}
and hence we can divide on both side.
∎

Lemma A.7
.

For all
n\in\mathbb{N}
and each
\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}
, define the vector of always-reporter indicators
A=(A_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
,
n_{A}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}
and
n_{A}^{1}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}
, where
D=\{D_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}\sim\textrm{CR}(n,n_{1})
. Define
\tau_{n}=n_{A}^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}\left(y_{i}(1)-y_{i}(0)\right)
.

Let
g:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
be an arbitrary function. Let
Z\sim\textrm{N}(0,1)
be a standard normal random variable independent of
D
. Define
\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}
as in (
14
) and
\operatorname{V}_{n,k}\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}\right)
as in (
53
).
10
10

10

When
k=1
or
k=n_{A}
, we shall abuse the notation and define
\operatorname{V}_{n,k}\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}\right)=\epsilon
for some arbitrary
\epsilon>0
. These events happen with probability approaching 0, as we shown in the proof.

For
n_{A}\geq 2
, we have the inequality, for every nonnegative real numbers
t
and
x
,

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\frac{t\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}-\tau_{n}\right)^{2}}{\operatorname{V}_{n,n_{A}^{1}}\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}\right)}+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq x\right)-\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(tZ^{2}+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq x\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2\left(1-r\right)}{rs^{2}(n-1)}+\sqrt{\frac{2}{r}}C_{\ref{eqn:53}}(\delta,r)\max_{a\in\{0,1\}}\max_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{\left|y_{i}(a)-\overline{y}_{A}(a)\right|}{\sqrt{n_{A}v^{a}_{A}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2\left(1-r\right)}{rs^{2}(n-1)}+\sqrt{\frac{2}{r}}C_{\ref{eqn:53}}(\delta,r)\times Bn_{A}^{-\frac{1}{4}},

where
B
is the constant defined in Assumption
2
-
(iii)
,
C_{\ref{eqn:53}}(\delta,r)
is a constant that depends on
\delta
and
r
defined in Assumption
2
-
(ii)
and Assumption
3
, respectively, and
s
is the constant defined in Assumption
2
-
(i)
.

Proof.

We suppress the dependence on
\theta_{n}^{w}
for simplicity. If
t=0
, the inequality trivially holds. We shall assume
t>0
.
We have the identity

\displaystyle\textnormal{E}\left[\mathds{1}\left\{\frac{t\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}-\tau_{n}\right)^{2}}{\operatorname{V}_{n,n_{A}^{1}}\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}\right)}+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq x\right\}\right]-\textnormal{E}\left[\mathds{1}\left\{tZ^{2}+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq x\right\}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\textnormal{E}\left[\textnormal{E}\left[\mathds{1}\left\{\frac{t\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}-\tau_{n}\right)^{2}}{\operatorname{V}_{n,n_{A}^{1}}\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}\right)}+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq x\right\}\Bigg|n_{A}^{1}\right]-\textnormal{E}\left[\mathds{1}\left\{tZ^{2}+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq x\right)\Bigg|n_{A}^{1}\right]\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\textnormal{E}\left[\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{t\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}-\tau_{n}\right)^{2}}{\operatorname{V}_{n,n_{A}^{1}}\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}\right)}+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq x\Bigg|n_{A}^{1}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(tZ^{2}+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq x\Bigg|n_{A}^{1}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\textnormal{E}\left[\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}-\tau_{n}\right)^{2}}{\operatorname{V}_{n,n_{A}^{1}}\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}\right)}\geq\frac{x-g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)}{t}\Bigg|n_{A}^{1}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(Z^{2}\geq\frac{x-g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)}{t}\Bigg|n_{A}^{1}\right)\right]

Recall that
n_{A}^{1}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}
and
n_{A}^{0}=n_{A}-n_{A}^{1}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(1-D_{i}\right)A_{i}
. Define events

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}=\left\{n_{A}^{1}\leq\frac{n_{A}n_{1}}{2n}\right\}\cup\left\{n_{A}^{0}\leq\frac{n_{A}n_{0}}{2n}\right\},

(56)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{c}=\left\{n_{A}^{1}>\frac{n_{A}n_{1}}{2n}\right\}\cap\left\{n_{A}^{0}>\frac{n_{A}n_{0}}{2n}\right\}.

(57)

On the event
\mathcal{E}^{c}
and by Assumption
3
, we have,

\min\left\{n_{A}^{1},n_{A}^{0}\right\}\geq\frac{rn_{A}}{2},\text{and, }\frac{1}{n_{A}}\min\left\{n_{A}^{1},n_{A}^{0}\right\}\geq\frac{r}{2}.

(58)

Hence on the event
\mathcal{E}^{c}
we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{V}_{n,n_{A}^{1}}\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\min\left\{\frac{\left(1-\delta\right)n_{A}^{1}}{n_{A}},\frac{\left(1-\delta\right)n_{A}^{0}}{n_{A}}\right\}\left(\frac{1}{n_{A}^{1}}v^{1}_{A}+\frac{1}{n_{A}^{0}}v^{0}_{A}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\left(1-\delta\right)r}{2}\left(\frac{1}{n_{A}^{1}}v^{1}_{A}+\frac{1}{n_{A}^{0}}v^{0}_{A}\right).

For a fixed
n_{A}^{1}
, the premise of Theorem
A.1
is satisfied with
c^{-2}=\left(1-\delta\right)r/2
. Conditioning on the event
\mathcal{E}^{c}
and for a fixed
n_{A}^{1}
, we have the calculation

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}-\tau_{n}\right)^{2}}{\operatorname{V}_{n,n_{A}^{1}}\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}\right)}\geq\frac{x-g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)}{t}\Bigg|n_{A}^{1}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(Z^{2}\geq\frac{x-g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)}{t}\Bigg|n_{A}^{1}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{q\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}-\tau_{n}\right)^{2}}{\operatorname{V}_{n,n_{A}^{1}}\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}\right)}\geq q\Bigg|n_{A}^{1}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(Z^{2}\geq q\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{q\in\mathbb{R}^{+}}\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\operatorname{V}_{n,n_{A}^{1}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}\right)\left|\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}-\tau_{n}\right|\geq\sqrt{q}\Bigg|n_{A}^{1}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(|Z|\geq\sqrt{q}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\operatorname{V}_{n,n_{A}^{1}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}\right)\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}-\tau_{n}\right)\leq t\Bigg|n_{A}^{1}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(Z\leq t\right)\right|

\displaystyle+\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\operatorname{V}_{n,n_{A}^{1}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}\right)\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}-\tau_{n}\right)\geq t\Bigg|n_{A}^{1}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(Z\geq t\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\ref{eqn:53}}(\delta,r)\max_{a\in\{0,1\}}\max_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{\left|y_{i}(a)-\overline{y}_{A}(a)\right|}{\sqrt{n_{A}^{a}v^{a}_{A}}},

(59)

by Theorem
A.1
and Lemma
A.6
-
(i)
, where the constant
C
may depend on
\delta
and
r
.

We have the following calculation:

\displaystyle\left|\textnormal{E}\left[\mathds{1}\left\{\frac{t\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}-\tau_{n}\right)^{2}}{\operatorname{V}_{n,n_{A}^{1}}\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}\right)}+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq x\right\}\right]-\textnormal{E}\left[\mathds{1}\left\{tZ^{2}+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq x\right\}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{E}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{E}^{c}\right)\times

\displaystyle\textnormal{E}\left[\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left.\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}-\tau_{n}\right)^{2}}{\operatorname{V}_{n,n_{A}^{1}}\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}\right)}\geq t\Bigg|n_{A}^{1}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(Z^{2}\geq t\Bigg|n_{A}^{1}\right)\right|\right|\mathcal{E}^{c}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{E}\right)+C_{\ref{eqn:53}}(\delta,r)\textnormal{E}\left[\left.\max_{a\in\{0,1\}}\max_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{\left|y_{i}(a)-\overline{y}_{A}(a)\right|}{\sqrt{n_{A}^{a}v^{a}_{A}}}\right|\mathcal{E}^{c}\right]\times\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{E}^{c}\right)

(60)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2\left(1-r\right)}{rs^{2}(n-1)}+\sqrt{\frac{2}{r}}C_{\ref{eqn:53}}(\delta,r)\max_{a\in\{0,1\}}\max_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{\left|y_{i}(a)-\overline{y}_{A}(a)\right|}{\sqrt{n_{A}v^{a}_{A}}},

(61)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2\left(1-r\right)}{rs^{2}(n-1)}+\sqrt{\frac{2}{r}}C_{\ref{eqn:53}}(\delta,r)\times Bn_{A}^{-\frac{1}{4}},

(62)

where (
60
) follows from (
59
), (
61
) follows from Lemma
A.3
and (
58
), and (
62
) follows from Lemma
A.6
-
(ii)
.
∎

A.3
Consistency of the Variance Estimator and Asymptotic Distributions of
\mathcal{T}^{0}_{n}
,
\mathcal{T}^{1}_{n}
and
\mathcal{T}^{2}_{n}

Recall the variance estimator defined in (
15
):

\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}\left(Y,D,A\right)=\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}\left(Y_{i}-\widehat{\tau}^{1}_{A}\right)^{2}}{\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}\right)^{2}}+\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-D_{i})A_{i}\left(Y_{i}-\widehat{\tau}^{0}_{A}\right)^{2}}{\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(1-D_{i}\right)A_{i}\right)^{2}},

where
\widehat{\tau}^{1}_{A}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}Y_{i}/\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}
and
\widehat{\tau}^{0}_{A}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(1-D_{i}\right)A_{i}Y_{i}/\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(1-D_{i}\right)A_{i}
.
Conditioning on the the event that
\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}=k
, the variance estimator can be expressed as:

\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n,k}\left(Y,D,A\right)=\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}\left(Y_{i}-\widehat{\tau}^{1}_{A,k}\right)^{2}}{k^{2}}+\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-D_{i})A_{i}\left(Y_{i}-\widehat{\tau}^{0}_{A,k}\right)^{2}}{\left(n_{A}-k\right)^{2}}

where,
\widehat{\tau}^{1}_{A,k}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}Y_{i}/k,\widehat{\tau}^{0}_{A,k}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(1-D_{i}\right)A_{i}Y_{i}/\left(n_{A}-k\right)
. For simplicity, we denote,

\widehat{v}^{1}_{A,k}=\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}\left(Y_{i}-\widehat{\tau}^{1}_{A,k}\right)^{2}}{k},\widehat{v}^{0}_{A,k}=\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(1-D_{i}\right)A_{i}\left(Y_{i}-\widehat{\tau}^{0}_{A,k}\right)^{2}}{n_{A}-k},

(63)

and the conditional variance estimator can be written as:

\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n,k}\left(Y,D,A\right)=\frac{1}{k}\widehat{v}^{1}_{A,k}+\frac{1}{n_{A}-k}\widehat{v}^{0}_{A,k}.

(64)

Define the target variance,

\widetilde{\operatorname{V}}_{n,k}\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}\right)=\frac{1}{k}v^{1}_{A}+\frac{1}{n_{A}-k}v^{0}_{A},

(65)

with
v^{1}_{A}
and
v^{0}_{A}
defined in (
51
). Lemma
A.8
provides a tail inequality for the variance estimator.

Lemma A.8
.

For all
n\in\mathbb{N}
and any
\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}
, let
\{\left(y_{i}(1),y_{i}(0)\right)\}_{i=1}^{n}
be the associated potential outcomes and
\{A_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
be the associated always-reporter indicators.
Denote
\mathcal{A}=\{i\in[n]:A_{i}=1\}
and
n_{A}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}
. Define
n_{A}^{1}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}
.

Consider the conditional variance estimator
\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n,k}
in (
64
), the variance target
\widetilde{\operatorname{V}}_{n,k}
(
65
).
Suppose
n_{A}\geq 2
. Let
k
be a positive integer with
k\in[1,n_{A}-1]
. For every
\epsilon\in(0,2]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\left.\left|\frac{\widetilde{\operatorname{V}}_{n,n_{A}^{1}}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n,n_{A}^{1}}}-1\right|\geq\epsilon\right|n_{A}^{1}=k\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\ref{eqn:ceb}}\left(\epsilon\right)\left(\frac{n_{A}-k}{k}+\frac{k}{n_{A}-k}\right)\frac{1}{n_{A}}\max_{a\in\{0,1\}}\frac{\max_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\left(y_{i}(a)-\overline{y}_{A}\left(a\right)\right)^{2}}{v^{a}_{A}}

\displaystyle+\frac{8(2+\epsilon)}{\epsilon}n_{A}^{-1}+\frac{2(2+\epsilon)}{\epsilon}n_{A}^{-1}\left(\frac{n_{A}-k}{k}+\frac{k}{n_{A}-k}\right),

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(C_{\ref{eqn:ceb}}\left(\epsilon\right)B^{2}+\frac{2(2+\epsilon)}{\epsilon}\right)n_{A}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\frac{n_{A}-k}{k}+\frac{k}{n_{A}-k}\right)+\frac{8(2+\epsilon)}{\epsilon}n_{A}^{-1},

where,

\displaystyle C_{\ref{eqn:ceb}}\left(\epsilon\right)=\left(\frac{8(2+\epsilon)}{\epsilon}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{2\left(2-\epsilon\right)}{\epsilon}\right)^{2}.

(66)

with the constant
B
defined in Assumption
2
-
(iii)
.

Proof.

Recall that by Lemma
A.4
, conditioning on the event
n_{A}^{1}=k
,
\left(D_{i}\right)_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\sim\textrm{CR}(n_{A},k)
. We suppress the dependence on
\theta_{n}^{w}
and the conditioning event for simplicity. By Assumption
(ii)
,
\widetilde{\operatorname{V}}_{n,k}
is positive. On the event where
\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n,k}=0
, we interpret
\widetilde{\operatorname{V}}_{n,k}/\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n,k}=\infty
.

We have the inequality:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\operatorname{V}}_{n,k}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n,k}}\geq 1+\epsilon\right)=\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\operatorname{V}}_{n,k}-\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n,k}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n,k}}\geq\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{k^{-1}v_{A}^{1}+(n_{A}-k)^{-1}v_{A}^{0}-k^{-1}\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{1}-(n_{A}-k)^{-1}\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{0}}{k^{-1}\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{1}+(n_{A}-k)^{-1}\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{0}}\geq\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{k^{-1}\left(v_{A}^{1}-\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{1}\right)}{k^{-1}\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{1}+(n_{A}-k)^{-1}\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{0}}\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{(n_{A}-k)^{-1}\left(v_{A}^{0}-\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{0}\right)}{k^{-1}\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{1}+(n_{A}-k)^{-1}\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{0}}\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\left(v_{A}^{1}-\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{1}\right)}{\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{1}}\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\left(v_{A}^{0}-\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{0}\right)}{\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{0}}\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right),

(67)

where we use the fact that
\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{1}
and
\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{0}
are nonnegative in (
67
). Similarly, we have,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\operatorname{V}}_{n,k}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n,k}}\leq 1-\epsilon\right)=\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\operatorname{V}}_{n,k}-\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n,k}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n,k}}\leq-\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{k^{-1}v_{A}^{1}+(n_{A}-k)^{-1}v_{A}^{0}-k^{-1}\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{1}-(n_{A}-k)^{-1}\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{0}}{k^{-1}\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{1}+(n_{A}-k)^{-1}\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{0}}\leq-\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{k^{-1}\left(v_{A}^{1}-\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{1}\right)}{k^{-1}\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{1}+(n_{A}-k)^{-1}\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{0}}\leq-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{(n_{A}-k)^{-1}\left(v_{A}^{0}-\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{0}\right)}{k^{-1}\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{1}+(n_{A}-k)^{-1}\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{0}}\leq-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\left(v_{A}^{1}-\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{1}\right)}{\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{1}}\leq-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\left(v_{A}^{0}-\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{0}\right)}{\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{0}}\leq-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right),

(68)

where we note that
\widetilde{\operatorname{V}}_{n,k}/\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n,k}\leq 1-\epsilon
implicitly implies that
\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n,k}>0
.
Combining (
67
) and (
68
) we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{\widetilde{\operatorname{V}}_{n,k}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n,k}}-1\right|\geq\epsilon\right)=\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\operatorname{V}}_{n,k}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n,k}}\geq 1+\epsilon\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\operatorname{V}}_{n,k}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n,k}}\leq 1-\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{v_{A}^{1}-\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{1}}{\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{1}}\right|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{v_{A}^{0}-\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{0}}{\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{0}}\right|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)

(69)

Our result follows from bounding the two terms in (
69
).
We bound the first term in (
69
). The bound for the second term follows from an analogous argument.

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\left|v_{A}^{1}-\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{1}\right|}{\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{1}}\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)=\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{v_{A}^{1}-\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{1}}{\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{1}}\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{v_{A}^{1}-\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{1}}{\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{1}}\leq-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{v_{A}^{1}}{\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{1}}\geq 1+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{v_{A}^{1}}{\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{1}}\leq 1-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right).

(70)

We have,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{v_{A}^{1}}{\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{1}}\geq 1+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)=\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{1}}{v_{A}^{1}}\leq\frac{2}{2+\epsilon}\right)=\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{1}-v_{A}^{1}}{v_{A}^{1}}\leq-\frac{\epsilon}{2+\epsilon}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{k^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}\left(Y_{i}-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)^{2}-\left(\widehat{\tau}^{1}_{A,k}-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)^{2}-v_{A}^{1}}{v_{A}^{1}}\leq-\frac{\epsilon}{2+\epsilon}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{k^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}\left(Y_{i}-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)^{2}-v^{1}_{A}}{v_{A}^{1}}\leq-\frac{\epsilon}{2\left(2+\epsilon\right)}\right)

(71)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}\left(-\frac{\left(\widehat{\tau}^{1}_{A,k}-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)^{2}}{v_{A}^{1}}\leq-\frac{\epsilon}{2\left(2+\epsilon\right)}\right),

(72)

where we use the fact that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{k}\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}\left(Y_{i}-\widehat{\tau}^{1}_{A,k}\right)^{2}=\frac{1}{k}\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}\left(Y_{i}-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)^{2}-\left(\widehat{\tau}^{1}_{A,k}-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)^{2}.

For (
72
), we have,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(-\frac{\left(\widehat{\tau}^{1}_{A,k}-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)^{2}}{v_{A}^{1}}\leq-\frac{\epsilon}{2\left(2+\epsilon\right)}\right)=\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\left(\widehat{\tau}^{1}_{A,k}-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)^{2}}{v_{A}^{1}}\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2\left(2+\epsilon\right)}\right)

(73)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2(2+\epsilon)}{\epsilon}\frac{\textnormal{E}\left[\left(\widehat{\tau}^{1}_{A,k}-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)^{2}\right]}{v_{A}^{1}}=\frac{2(2+\epsilon)}{\epsilon}\frac{\textrm{V}\left(\widehat{\tau}^{1}_{A,k}\right)}{v^{1}_{A}}=\frac{2\left(2+\epsilon\right)}{\epsilon}\frac{n_{A}-k}{n_{A}k},

(74)

where the last equality follows from

\textrm{V}\left(\widehat{\tau}^{1}_{A,k}\right)=\frac{n_{A}-k}{n_{A}k}\frac{1}{n_{A}-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\left(y_{i}(1)-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)^{2}=\frac{n_{A}-k}{n_{A}k}v^{1}_{A}.

(75)

Notice we can write:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{k}\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}\left(Y_{i}-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)^{2}-v^{1}_{A}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{n_{A}}{\left(n_{A}-1\right)k}\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}\left(Y_{i}-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)^{2}-v^{1}_{A}-\frac{1}{\left(n_{A}-1\right)k}\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}\left(Y_{i}-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)^{2}

Now,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{k^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}\left(Y_{i}-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)^{2}-v^{1}_{A}}{v_{A}^{1}}\leq-\frac{\epsilon}{2\left(2+\epsilon\right)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\frac{n_{A}}{k}\frac{1}{n_{A}-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}\left(Y_{i}-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)^{2}-v^{1}_{A}}{v_{A}^{1}}\leq-\frac{\epsilon}{4\left(2+\epsilon\right)}\right)

(76)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{-\frac{1}{\left(n_{A}-1\right)k}\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}\left(Y_{i}-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)^{2}}{v_{A}^{1}}\leq-\frac{\epsilon}{4\left(2+\epsilon\right)}\right)

(77)

A bound for (
76
) follows from the calculation

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\frac{n_{A}}{k}\frac{1}{n_{A}-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}\left(Y_{i}-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)^{2}-v^{1}_{A}}{v_{A}^{1}}\leq-\frac{\epsilon}{4\left(2+\epsilon\right)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{4(2+\epsilon)}{\epsilon}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{n_{A}}{n_{A}-1}\right)^{2}\left(v^{1}_{A}\right)^{-2}\textrm{V}\left(\frac{1}{k}\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}\left(Y_{i}-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)^{2}\right)

(78)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{4(2+\epsilon)}{\epsilon}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{n_{A}}{n_{A}-1}\right)^{2}\left(v^{1}_{A}\right)^{-2}\frac{n_{A}-k}{kn_{A}}\frac{1}{n_{A}-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\left(y_{i}(1)-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)^{4}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{4(2+\epsilon)}{\epsilon}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{n_{A}}{n_{A}-1}\right)^{2}\frac{n_{A}-k}{kn_{A}}\frac{\max_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\left(y_{i}(1)-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)^{2}}{v^{1}_{A}}

(79)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{4(2+\epsilon)}{\epsilon}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{n_{A}}{n_{A}-1}\right)^{2}\frac{n_{A}-k}{kn_{A}}B^{2}n_{A}^{\frac{1}{2}},

(80)

where (
78
) uses the Chebyshev inequality and the fact that

\textnormal{E}\left[\frac{n_{A}}{k}\frac{1}{n_{A}-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}\left(Y_{i}-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)^{2}\right]=v_{A}^{1},

(81)

(
79
) uses the fact that

\frac{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\left(y_{i}(1)-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)^{4}}{n_{A}-1}\leq\frac{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\left(y_{i}(1)-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)^{2}}{n_{A}-1}\times\max_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\left(y_{i}(1)-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)^{2},

and (
80
) uses Lemma
A.6
-
(ii)
. A bound for (
77
) follows from the calculation

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{-\frac{1}{k\left(n_{A}-1\right)}\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}\left(Y_{i}-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)^{2}}{v_{A}^{1}}\leq-\frac{\epsilon}{4\left(2+\epsilon\right)}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\frac{1}{k\left(n_{A}-1\right)}\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}\left(Y_{i}-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)^{2}}{v_{A}^{1}}\geq\frac{\epsilon}{4\left(2+\epsilon\right)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{4\left(2+\epsilon\right)}{\epsilon}\frac{\frac{1}{n_{A}\left(n_{A}-1\right)}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\left(y_{i}(1)-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)^{2}}{v_{A}^{1}}=\frac{4\left(2+\epsilon\right)}{n_{A}\epsilon},

by the Markov inequality and
81
. Collecting terms we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{v_{A}^{1}}{\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{1}}\geq 1+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{4(2+\epsilon)}{\epsilon}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{n_{A}}{n_{A}-1}\right)^{2}\frac{n_{A}-k}{kn_{A}}B^{2}n_{A}^{\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{4\left(2+\epsilon\right)}{n_{A}\epsilon}+\frac{2\left(2+\epsilon\right)}{\epsilon}\frac{n_{A}-k}{n_{A}k}.

For the second term in (
70
). We use the premise that
\epsilon\leq 2
and

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{v_{A}^{1}}{\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{1}}\leq 1-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)=\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{v_{A}^{1}}{\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{1}}\leq\frac{2-\epsilon}{2}\right)=\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{1}}{v_{A}^{1}}\geq\frac{2}{2-\epsilon}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{k^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}\left(Y_{i}-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)^{2}-\left(\widehat{\tau}^{1}_{A,k}-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)^{2}-v_{A}^{1}}{v_{A}^{1}}\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2-\epsilon}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{k^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}\left(Y_{i}-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)^{2}-v_{A}^{1}}{v_{A}^{1}}\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2-\epsilon}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\frac{n_{A}}{\left(n_{A}-1\right)k}\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}\left(Y_{i}-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)^{2}-v^{1}_{A}}{v_{A}^{1}}\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2-\epsilon}\right)

(82)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left(\frac{\frac{n_{A}}{\left(n_{A}-1\right)k}\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}\left(Y_{i}-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)^{2}-v^{1}_{A}}{v_{A}^{1}}\right)^{2}\geq\left(\frac{\epsilon}{2-\epsilon}\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{2-\epsilon}{\epsilon}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{n_{A}}{n_{A}-1}\right)^{2}\left(v^{1}_{A}\right)^{-2}\textrm{V}\left(\frac{1}{k}\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}\left(Y_{i}-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)^{2}\right)

(83)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{2-\epsilon}{\epsilon}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{n_{A}}{n_{A}-1}\right)^{2}\left(v^{1}_{A}\right)^{-2}\frac{n_{A}-k}{kn_{A}}\frac{1}{n_{A}-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\left(y_{i}(1)-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)^{4}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{2-\epsilon}{\epsilon}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{n_{A}}{n_{A}-1}\right)^{2}\frac{n_{A}-k}{kn_{A}}\frac{\max_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\left(y_{i}(1)-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)^{2}}{v^{1}_{A}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{2-\epsilon}{\epsilon}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{n_{A}}{n_{A}-1}\right)^{2}\frac{n_{A}-k}{kn_{A}}B^{2}n_{A}^{\frac{1}{2}},

where in (
82
) follows because
\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}\left(Y_{i}-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)^{2}
is nonnegative and
n_{A}\left(n_{A}-1\right)^{-1}\geq 1
for
n_{A}\geq 2
and rest calculations are similar to the bound for (
76
).
Hence for
\epsilon\leq 2
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\left|v_{A}^{1}-\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{1}\right|}{\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{1}}\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)\leq\left(\frac{4(2+\epsilon)}{\epsilon}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{n_{A}}{n_{A}-1}\right)^{2}\frac{n_{A}-k}{kn_{A}}B^{2}n_{A}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{4\left(2+\epsilon\right)}{n_{A}\epsilon}+\frac{2\left(2+\epsilon\right)}{\epsilon}\frac{n_{A}-k}{n_{A}k}+\left(\frac{2-\epsilon}{\epsilon}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{n_{A}}{n_{A}-1}\right)^{2}\frac{n_{A}-k}{kn_{A}}B^{2}n_{A}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

For
n_{A}\geq 2
, we have
0\leq n_{A}/(n_{A}-1)\leq 2
. Hence we can simplify,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\left|v_{A}^{1}-\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{1}\right|}{\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{1}}\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\ref{eqn:ceb}}(\epsilon)B^{2}\frac{n_{A}-k}{k}n_{A}^{-\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{4(2+\epsilon)}{\epsilon}n_{A}^{-1}+\frac{2(2+\epsilon)}{\epsilon}\frac{n_{A}-k}{k}n_{A}^{-1},

where

\displaystyle C_{\ref{eqn:ceb}}(\epsilon)=\left(\frac{8(2+\epsilon)}{\epsilon}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{2\left(2-\epsilon\right)}{\epsilon}\right)^{2}.

By symmetry, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\left|v_{A}^{0}-\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{0}\right|}{\widehat{v}_{A,k}^{0}}\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\ref{eqn:ceb}}(\epsilon)B^{2}\frac{k}{n_{A}-k}n_{A}^{-\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{4(2+\epsilon)}{\epsilon}n_{A}^{-1}+\frac{2(2+\epsilon)}{\epsilon}\frac{k}{n_{A}-k}n_{A}^{-1}.

(84)

Combining these two bounds gives us the result in the lemma.
∎

Corollary A.9
.

Under the setup of Lemma
A.8
, we have, for
n\geq 2
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\left|\frac{\widetilde{\textrm{V}}_{n,n_{A}^{1}}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}}-1\right|\geq\epsilon\right)=\textnormal{E}\left[\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\left|\frac{\widetilde{\textrm{V}}_{n,n_{A}^{1}}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n,n_{A}^{1}}}-1\right|\geq\epsilon\bigg|n_{A}^{1}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\ref{constant:2}}(r,s,\epsilon)n^{-1}+C_{\ref{constant:3}}\left(\epsilon,r\right)\frac{1}{n_{A}}\max_{a\in\{0,1\}}\frac{\max_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\left(y_{i}(a)-\overline{y}_{A}\left(a\right)\right)^{2}}{v^{a}_{A}}

(85)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\ref{constant:2}}(r,s,\epsilon)n^{-1}+C_{\ref{constant:3}}\left(\epsilon,r\right)B^{2}n_{A}^{-\frac{1}{2}}

(86)

where
C_{\ref{constant:3}}\left(\epsilon,r\right)
is defined as

C_{\ref{constant:3}}(\epsilon,r)=C_{\ref{eqn:ceb}}\left(\epsilon\right)\frac{2\left(2-r\right)}{r},

(87)

with
C_{\ref{eqn:ceb}}\left(\epsilon\right)
defined in (
66
), and
C_{\ref{constant:2}}(r,s,\epsilon)
is defined as

C_{\ref{constant:2}}\left(r,s,\epsilon\right)=\frac{4\left(1-r\right)}{rs^{2}}+\frac{8\left(2+\epsilon\right)}{\epsilon s}+\frac{2\left(2+\epsilon\right)}{\epsilon}\frac{2\left(2-r\right)}{rs}

(88)

with constants
r
,
s
and
B
defined in Assumption-
3
, Assumption
2
-
(i)
and Assumption
2
-
(iii)
, respectively.

Proof.

We suppress the dependence on
\theta_{n}^{w}
and the conditioning event for simplicity. Define events
\mathcal{E}
and
\mathcal{E}^{c}
as in (
56
) and (
57
).
Note on the event
\mathcal{E}^{c}
, we have the inequality,

n_{A}^{1}>\frac{n_{A}n_{1}}{2n}\geq\frac{rn_{A}}{2},n_{A}^{0}>\frac{n_{A}n_{0}}{2n}\geq\frac{rn_{A}}{2}.

n_{A}^{1}=n_{A}-n_{A}^{0}<n_{A}-\frac{rn_{A}}{2}=\left(1-\frac{r}{2}\right)n_{A},

n_{A}^{0}=n_{A}-n_{A}^{1}<n_{A}-\frac{rn_{A}}{2}=\left(1-\frac{r}{2}\right)n_{A}.

Hence, conditioning on the event
\mathcal{E}^{c}
, we have

\frac{n_{A}^{1}}{n_{A}^{0}}\in\left(\frac{r/2}{1-r/2},\frac{1-r/2}{r/2}\right),\frac{n_{A}^{0}}{n_{A}^{1}}\in\left(\frac{r/2}{1-r/2},\frac{1-r/2}{r/2}\right).

(89)

The result follows by

\displaystyle\textnormal{E}\left[\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{\widetilde{\textrm{V}}_{n,n_{A}^{1}}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n,n_{A}^{1}}}-1\right|\geq\epsilon\bigg|n_{A}^{1}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{E}\right)+\textnormal{E}\left[\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{\widetilde{\textrm{V}}_{n,n_{A}^{1}}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n,n_{A}^{1}}}-1\right|\geq\epsilon\bigg|n_{A}^{1}\right)\bigg|\mathcal{E}^{c}\right]\times\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{E}^{c}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{E}\right)+

\displaystyle\textnormal{E}\left[C_{\ref{eqn:ceb}}\left(\epsilon\right)\left(\frac{n_{A}^{0}}{n_{A}^{1}}+\frac{n_{A}^{1}}{n_{A}^{0}}\right)\frac{1}{n_{A}}\max_{a\in\{0,1\}}\frac{\max_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\left(y_{i}(a)-\overline{y}_{A}\left(a\right)\right)^{2}}{v^{a}_{A}}\bigg|\mathcal{E}^{c}\right]+

\displaystyle\textnormal{E}\left[\frac{8(2+\epsilon)}{\epsilon}n_{A}^{-1}+\frac{2(2+\epsilon)}{\epsilon}n_{A}^{-1}\left(\frac{n_{A}^{0}}{n_{A}^{1}}+\frac{n_{A}^{1}}{n_{A}^{0}}\right)\bigg|\mathcal{E}^{c}\right],

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2(1-r)}{rs^{2}(n-1)}+C_{\ref{eqn:ceb}}\left(\epsilon\right)\frac{2\left(2-r\right)}{r}\frac{1}{n_{A}}\max_{a\in\{0,1\}}\frac{\max_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\left(y_{i}(a)-\overline{y}_{A}\left(a\right)\right)^{2}}{v^{a}_{A}}

\displaystyle+\frac{8\left(2+\epsilon\right)}{\epsilon}n_{A}^{-1}+\frac{2\left(2+\epsilon\right)}{\epsilon}\frac{2\left(2-r\right)}{r}n_{A}^{-1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2(1-r)}{rs^{2}(n-1)}+C_{\ref{eqn:ceb}}\left(\epsilon\right)B^{2}\frac{2\left(2-r\right)}{r}n_{A}^{-\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{8\left(2+\epsilon\right)}{\epsilon}n_{A}^{-1}+\frac{2\left(2+\epsilon\right)}{\epsilon}\frac{2\left(2-r\right)}{r}n_{A}^{-1}

where the second inequality follows from Lemma
A.8
with the constant
C(\epsilon)
defined in (
66
) and the third inequality follows from Lemma
A.3
and (
89
). Constants
r
,
s
and
B
are defined in Assumption-
3
, Assumption
2
-
(i)
and Assumption
2
-
(ii)
, respectively.

For each
\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}
, we have
n_{A}^{-1}\leq s^{-1}n^{-1}
. We write, for
n\geq 2

\displaystyle\frac{2(1-r)}{rs^{2}(n-1)}n^{-1}n+\frac{8\left(2+\epsilon\right)}{\epsilon}s^{-1}n^{-1}+\frac{2\left(2+\epsilon\right)}{\epsilon}\frac{2\left(2-r\right)}{r}s^{-1}n^{-1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{4\left(1-r\right)}{rs^{2}}+\frac{8\left(2+\epsilon\right)}{\epsilon}s^{-1}+\frac{2\left(2+\epsilon\right)}{\epsilon}\frac{2\left(2-r\right)}{r}s^{-1}\right)n^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{\ref{constant:2}}(r,s,\epsilon)n^{-1}.

∎

Anticipating the proof in Lemma
A.23
and recalling the definition of
\widehat{v}_{A}^{1,k}
and
\widehat{v}_{A}^{0,k}
in (
64
), we note that a similar calculation can be carried out for
\epsilon\leq 1

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{v_{A}^{1}}{\widehat{v}_{A,n_{A}^{1}}^{1}}-1\right|\geq\epsilon\right)=\textnormal{E}\left[\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{v_{A}^{1}}{\widehat{v}_{A,n_{A}^{1}}^{1}}-1\right|\geq\epsilon\bigg|n_{A}^{1}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{E}\right)+\textnormal{E}\left[\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{v_{A}^{1}}{\widehat{v}_{A,n_{A}^{1}}^{1}}-1\right|\geq\frac{2\epsilon}{2}\bigg|n_{A}^{1}\right)\bigg|\mathcal{E}^{c}\right]\times\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{E}^{c}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{E}\right)+\textnormal{E}\left[C_{\ref{eqn:ceb}}\left(2\epsilon\right)B^{2}\frac{n_{A}^{0}}{n_{A}^{1}}n_{A}^{-\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{4(1+\epsilon)}{\epsilon}n_{A}^{-1}+\frac{2(1+\epsilon)}{\epsilon}\frac{n_{A}^{0}}{n_{A}^{1}}n_{A}^{-1}\bigg|\mathcal{E}^{c}\right],

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2\left(1-r\right)}{rs^{2}\left(n-1\right)}+C_{\ref{eqn:ceb}}\left(2\epsilon\right)B^{2}\frac{2-r}{r}n_{A}^{-\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{8\left(2+\epsilon\right)}{\epsilon}n_{A}^{-1}+\frac{2\left(2+\epsilon\right)}{\epsilon}\frac{2-r}{r}n_{A}^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}),

(90)

where the second inequality is by (
84
) and the third inequality is by Lemma
A.3
and (
89
). A similar calculation holds for
\widehat{v}_{A,n_{A}^{0}}^{0}
.

Lemma A.10
.

For all
n\in\mathbb{N}
and any
\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}
, let
\{\left(y_{i}(1),y_{i}(0)\right)\}_{i=1}^{n}
be the associated potential outcomes and
\{A_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
be the associated always-reporter indicators. Denote
D=\{D_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}\sim\textrm{CR}(n,n_{1})
,

and

. Define
\tau_{n}=n_{A}^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\left(y_{i}(1)-y_{i}(0)\right)
.

Consider the variance estimator

in (

) and the Hajek estimator

, that is independent from

. There exists a positive integer

such that for all

, every

and

,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\frac{\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}-\tau_{n}\right)^{2}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}}+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq t\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(Z^{2}\left(1+\epsilon\right)+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq t\right)

\displaystyle+C_{\ref{constant:2}}(r,s,\epsilon)n^{-1}+C_{\ref{constant:3}}\left(\epsilon,r\right)B^{2}n_{A}^{-\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{2\left(1-r\right)}{rs^{2}(n-1)}+\sqrt{\frac{2}{r}}C_{\ref{eqn:53}}(\delta,r)\times Bn_{A}^{-\frac{1}{4}}

where the constant

is defined in (

),
C_{\ref{constant:2}}(r,s,\epsilon)
is defined in (
88
),
C_{\ref{eqn:53}}(\delta,r)
is defined in Lemma
A.7
, and constants

,

,
\delta
and

are defined in Assumption
3
, Assumption
2
-
(i)
, Assumption
2
-
(ii)
and Assumption
2
-
(iii)
, respectively.

Proof.

We suppress the dependence on
\theta_{n}^{w}
and the conditioning event for simplicity. Pick
N=\lceil 2s^{-1}\rceil
. We have
n_{A}\geq sn\geq 2
for all
n\geq N
. Recall the definitions of

) and (

.

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}-\tau_{n}\right)^{2}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}}+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq t\right)=\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}-\tau_{n}\right)^{2}}{\textrm{V}_{n,n_{A}^{1}}}\frac{\textrm{V}_{n,n_{A}^{1}}}{\widetilde{\textrm{V}}_{n,n_{A}^{1}}}\frac{\widetilde{\textrm{V}}_{n,n_{A}^{1}}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}}+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq t\right)

(91)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\textnormal{E}\left[\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}-\tau_{n}\right)^{2}}{\textrm{V}_{n,n_{A}^{1}}}\frac{\widetilde{\textrm{V}}_{n,n_{A}^{1}}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n,n_{A}^{1}}}+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq t\bigg|n_{A}^{1}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\textnormal{E}\left[\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}-\tau_{n}\right)^{2}}{\textrm{V}_{n,n_{A}^{1}}}\frac{\widetilde{\textrm{V}}_{n,n_{A}^{1}}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n,n_{A}^{1}}}+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq t,\frac{\widetilde{\textrm{V}}_{n,n_{A}^{1}}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n,n_{A}^{1}}}\leq 1+\epsilon\bigg|n_{A}^{1}\right)\right]

(92)

\displaystyle+\textnormal{E}\left[\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\textrm{V}}_{n,n_{A}^{1}}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n,n_{A}^{1}}}\geq 1+\epsilon\bigg|n_{A}^{1}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\textnormal{E}\left[\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}-\tau_{n}\right)^{2}}{\textrm{V}_{n,n_{A}^{1}}}\left(1+\epsilon\right)+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq t\bigg|n_{A}^{1}\right)\right]+\textnormal{E}\left[\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\textrm{V}}_{n,n_{A}^{1}}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n,n_{A}^{1}}}\geq 1+\epsilon\bigg|n_{A}^{1}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\textnormal{E}\left[\mathbb{P}\left(Z^{2}\left(1+\epsilon\right)+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq t\bigg|n_{A}^{1}\right)\right]+\textnormal{E}\left[\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\textrm{V}}_{n,n_{A}^{1}}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n,n_{A}^{1}}}\geq 1+\epsilon\bigg|n_{A}^{1}\right)\right]

\displaystyle+\Bigg(\textnormal{E}\left[\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}-\tau_{n}\right)^{2}}{\textrm{V}_{n,n_{A}^{1}}}\left(1+\epsilon\right)+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq t\bigg|n_{A}^{1}\right)\right]

\displaystyle-\textnormal{E}\left[\mathbb{P}\left(Z^{2}\left(1+\epsilon\right)+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq t\bigg|n_{A}^{1}\right)\right]\Bigg)

where for (
91
) we use the fact that
\textrm{V}_{n,n_{A}^{1}}/\widetilde{\textrm{V}}_{n,n_{A}^{1}}\leq 1
. The results follow by applying Lemma
