and Corollary
A.9
.
∎

Note because for all
\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}
, we have
n_{A}^{-1}\leq s^{-1}n^{-1}

For
n\geq 2
, we can simplify the upper bounds by

\displaystyle C_{\ref{constant:2}}(r,s,\epsilon)n^{-1}+C_{\ref{constant:3}}\left(\epsilon,r\right)B^{2}n_{A}^{-\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{2\left(1-r\right)}{rs^{2}(n-1)}+\sqrt{\frac{2}{r}}C_{\ref{eqn:53}}(\delta,r)\times Bn_{A}^{-\frac{1}{4}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\ref{constant:2}}(r,s,\epsilon)n^{-1}+C_{\ref{constant:3}}\left(\epsilon,r\right)B^{2}n_{A}^{-\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{4\left(1-r\right)}{rs^{2}}n^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{2}{r}}C_{\ref{eqn:53}}(\delta,r)\times Bn_{A}^{-\frac{1}{4}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{6\left(1-r\right)}{rs^{2}}+\frac{8\left(2+\epsilon\right)}{\epsilon s}+\frac{2\left(2+\epsilon\right)}{\epsilon}\frac{2\left(2-r\right)}{rs}\right)n^{-1}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle C_{\ref{constant:3}}\left(\epsilon,r\right)B^{2}n_{A}^{-\frac{1}{2}}+\sqrt{\frac{2}{r}}C_{\ref{eqn:53}}(\delta,r)\times Bn_{A}^{-\frac{1}{4}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{6\left(1-r\right)}{rs^{2}}+\frac{8\left(2+\epsilon\right)}{\epsilon s}+\frac{2\left(2+\epsilon\right)}{\epsilon}\frac{2\left(2-r\right)}{rs}\right)n^{-1}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle C_{\ref{constant:3}}\left(\epsilon,r\right)B^{2}s^{-\frac{1}{2}}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}+\sqrt{\frac{2}{r}}C_{\ref{eqn:53}}(\delta,r)\times Bs^{-\frac{1}{4}}n^{-\frac{1}{4}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\ref{constant:1}}(r,s,\epsilon,B,\delta)n^{-\frac{1}{4}}

with

\displaystyle\begin{split}C_{\ref{constant:1}}(r,s,\epsilon,B,\delta)&=\frac{6\left(1-r\right)}{rs^{2}}+\frac{8\left(2+\epsilon\right)}{\epsilon s}+\frac{2\left(2+\epsilon\right)}{\epsilon}\frac{2\left(2-r\right)}{rs}+\\
&C_{\ref{constant:3}}\left(\epsilon,r\right)B^{2}s^{-\frac{1}{2}}+\sqrt{\frac{2}{r}}C_{\ref{eqn:53}}(\delta,r)\times Bs^{-\frac{1}{4}}.\end{split}

(93)

We shall use this constant for the remaining calculations.

Corollary A.11
.

Under the same setup as in Lemma
A.10
, suppose

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{\theta^{w}_{n}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}}\textnormal{E}_{\theta^{w}_{n}}\left[\left|g(n_{A}^{1})\right|\right]\leq C<\infty

(94)

for some positive constant
C
.
For all
t\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sup_{\theta^{w}_{n}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{w}_{n}}\left(\frac{\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}-\tau_{n}\right)^{2}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}}+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq t\right)-\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(Z^{2}+g(n_{A}^{1})\geq t\right)\right)\leq 0.

Proof.

For
\epsilon\in(0,2]
and
t\in\mathbb{R}
, the bound in Lemma
A.10
applies for all parameters
\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}
and all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we shall write, for simplicity,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\frac{\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}-\tau_{n}\right)^{2}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}}+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq t\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(Z^{2}\left(1+\epsilon\right)+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq t\right)+C_{\ref{constant:1}}(r,s,\epsilon,B,\delta)n^{-\frac{1}{4}}.

Hence we have, for
\epsilon\in(0,2]
and
t\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\frac{\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}-\tau_{n}\right)^{2}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}}+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq t\right)-\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(Z^{2}+g(n_{A}^{1})\geq t\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(Z^{2}\left(1+\epsilon\right)+g(n_{A}^{1})\geq t\right)-\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(Z^{2}+g(n_{A}^{1})\geq t\right)\right)

\displaystyle+C_{\ref{constant:1}}(r,s,\epsilon,B,\delta)n^{-\frac{1}{4}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{\epsilon\left(|t|+C\right)}{2\pi\left(1+\epsilon\right)}}+C_{\ref{constant:1}}(r,s,\epsilon,B,\delta)n^{-\frac{1}{4}},

(95)

where for (
95
) we used the fact that

\sup_{\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(Z^{2}\left(1+\epsilon\right)+g(n_{A}^{1})\geq t\right)-\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(Z^{2}+g(n_{A}^{1})\geq t\right)\right)\leq\sqrt{\frac{\epsilon\left(|t|+C\right)}{2\pi\left(1+\epsilon\right)}},

which is used in Lemma
A.26
.
Taking
\limsup_{n\to\infty}
followed by
\lim\sup_{\epsilon\to 0}
yields the stated result.
∎

We note that
\mathcal{T}_{n}^{0}
,
\mathcal{T}_{n}^{1}
and
\mathcal{T}_{n}^{2}
defined in (
17
), (
19
) and (
20
) has the form

\frac{\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}-\tau_{n}\right)^{2}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}}+g_{i}\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)

with associated the
g_{i}
,
i\in\{0,1,2\}
, functions:

\displaystyle g_{0}(n_{A}^{1})=0,g_{1}(n_{A}^{1})=\textrm{V}_{n}^{-1}(A)\left(\frac{n_{A}^{1}}{n_{1}}-\frac{n_{A}-n_{A}^{1}}{n_{0}}\right)^{2}

(96)

\displaystyle g_{2}(n_{A}^{1})=\textrm{V}_{n}^{-1}(A)\left(\left\lfloor\frac{n_{A}^{1}}{n_{1}}-\frac{n_{A}-n_{A}^{1}}{n_{0}}\right\rfloor_{-}\right)^{2}.

(97)

Theorem
A.12
characterizes the asymptotic distributions of
\mathcal{T}_{n}^{0}
,
\mathcal{T}_{n}^{1}
, and
\mathcal{T}_{n}^{2}
, evaluated at the true always-reporter table
A
implied by each
\theta_{n}^{w}
.

Theorem A.12
.

Under the same setup as in Lemma
A.10
, for every
t\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\mathcal{T}_{n}^{i}\geq t\right)-\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(Z^{2}+g_{i}(n_{A}^{1})\geq t\right)\right)\leq 0.

for
i\in\{0,1,2\}
, where
\mathcal{T}_{n}^{0}
,
\mathcal{T}_{n}^{1}
and
\mathcal{T}_{n}^{2}
are defined in (
17
), (
19
) and (
20
) respectively.

Proof.

It is clear that
\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{\theta_{n}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}}\textnormal{E}_{\theta_{n}}\left[\left|g_{0}(n_{A}^{1})\right|\right]=0
. Since
0\leq g_{2}(n_{A}^{1})\leq g_{1}(n_{A}^{1})
, we only need to verify the condition for
g_{1}(n_{A}^{1})
. Now,

\textnormal{E}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left[g_{1}(n_{A}^{1})\right]=\textrm{V}_{n}^{-1}\left(A\right)\textnormal{E}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left[\left(\frac{n_{A}^{1}}{n_{1}}-\frac{n_{A}-n_{A}^{1}}{n_{0}}\right)^{2}\right]=\textrm{V}_{n}^{-1}\left(A\right)\textrm{V}_{n}\left(A\right)=1,

for all
\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}
and all
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Then the conclusion follows from Corollary
A.11
.
∎

A.4
Randomization Critical Value

This section analyzes the properties of randomization critical values. Our main result, Theorem
A.25
, establishes their convergence and thus justifies the asymptotic validity of using the randomization-based critical value.

Section
A.4.2
states some properties of the quantiles of the random variables
Z^{2}+g(n_{A}^{1})
, where
Z\sim\mathbb{N}(0,1)
. Section
A.4.3
uses these results to prove Theorem
A.25
.

A.4.1
Preliminary Notations

We use the shorthand notation
\Theta^{w}_{n}=\Theta^{w}_{n}(\delta,s,r,B)
, as defined in (
25
). We typically denote an element in
\Theta_{n}^{w}
by
\theta_{n}^{w}
.

We use
\chi^{2}_{1-\alpha}
to denote the
(1-\alpha)
-quantile of a chi-square
random variable with one degree of freedom. Note that if

Z\sim\textnormal{N}(0,1)
, then
Z^{2}
follows a chi-square distribution with
one degree of freedom.

Let
Y=(Y_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
denote the observed outcomes and
A=(A_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
a reporting table.
Let
D^{*}=(D_{i}^{*})_{i=1}^{n}\sim\mathrm{CR}(n,n_{1})
be an independently generated treatment assignment vector. We define the test-statistic based on the randomization distribution as:

Z^{*}=\frac{\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}*}\left(D^{*}\right)}{\sqrt{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}*}_{n}\left(D^{*}\right)}},

(98)

where,

\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}*}\left(D^{*}\right)=\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}D^{*}_{i}A_{i}Y_{i}}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}D^{*}_{i}A_{i}}-\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(1-D^{*}_{i}\right)A_{i}Y_{i}}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(1-D^{*}_{i}\right)A_{i}},

and

\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}*}_{n}\left(D^{*}\right)=\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}D^{*}_{i}A_{i}\left(Y_{i}-\widehat{\tau}^{1*}_{A}\right)^{2}}{\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}D^{*}_{i}A_{i}\right)^{2}}+\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-D^{*}_{i})A_{i}\left(Y_{i}-\widehat{\tau}^{0*}_{A}\right)^{2}}{\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(1-D^{*}_{i}\right)A_{i}\right)^{2}},

with,

\displaystyle\widehat{\tau}^{1*}_{A}=\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}D^{*}_{i}A_{i}Y_{i}}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}D^{*}_{i}A_{i}},\text{ and, }\widehat{\tau}^{0*}_{A}=\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(1-D^{*}_{i}\right)A_{i}Y_{i}}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(1-D^{*}_{i}\right)A_{i}}.

We also define

n_{A}^{1*}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}^{*}A_{i},n_{A}^{0*}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(1-D_{i}^{*}\right)A_{i}.

(99)

Given a measurable function
g
, define the randomization distribution function of
(Z^{*})^{2}+g(n_{A}^{1*})
based on the observed data by

t\;\longmapsto\;\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\!\left(\left(Z^{*}\right)^{2}+g\left(n_{A}^{1*}\right)\leq t\,\middle|\,D^{\textnormal{obs}}\right),\qquad t\in\mathbb{R}.

(100)

We write “
|\,D^{\textnormal{obs}})
” to emphasize the fact that the distribution is conditioned on the realized assignments. Unless noted otherwise, we define
n_{A}^{1}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}
.

A.4.2
Properties of the Quantiles

Lemma
A.13
states that the quantiles of
Z^{2}+g(n_{A}^{1})
are uniformly bounded in
n
, under suitable regularity conditions on
g
.

Lemma A.13
.

Let
g:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
be a function such that

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}}\textnormal{E}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left[\left|g(n_{A}^{1})\right|\right]\leq C<\infty,

for a positive constant
C
. Given an
\alpha\in\left(0,1\right)
, let
q_{n}^{1-\alpha}
be the
\left(1-\alpha\right)
-th quantile of the random variable
Z^{2}+g(n_{A}^{1})
, where
Z\sim\textnormal{N}\left(0,1\right)
is independent of
n_{A}^{1}
. We have

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}}q_{n}^{1-\alpha}\leq\chi^{2}_{1-\alpha/2}+\frac{2C}{\alpha}

(101)

Proof.

We suppress the dependence on
\theta_{n}^{w}
. We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(Z^{2}+g(n_{A}^{1})\geq\chi^{2}_{1-\alpha/2}+\frac{C}{\alpha/2}\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\left(Z^{2}\geq\chi^{2}_{1-\alpha/2}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq\frac{C}{\alpha/2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(Z^{2}\geq\chi^{2}_{1-\alpha/2}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\left|g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\right|\geq\frac{2C}{\alpha}\right)\leq\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{\alpha}{2}\frac{\textnormal{E}\left[\left|g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\right|\right]}{C}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\alpha.

Hence we must have
q_{n}^{1-\alpha}\leq\chi^{2}_{1-\alpha/2}+2C/\alpha
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
∎

We note that
Z^{2}
with
Z\sim\textnormal{N}\left(0,1\right)
follows a Chi-squared
\chi^{2}_{1}
distribution with one degree of freedom.
Z^{2}
has a density function:

f_{\chi^{2}_{1}}(t)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}t^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-\frac{t}{2}},t\in\left(0,\infty\right),

which is strictly decreasing on its support.

Lemma A.14
.

Consider a vector of always–reporter indicators
A=\left(A_{i}\right)_{i=1}^{n}
, and a completely randomized design
D=\left(D_{i}\right)_{i=1}^{n}\sim\mathrm{CR}(n,n_{1})
. Let
n_{0}=n-n_{1}
and
n_{A}^{1}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}
. Let
Z\sim\textrm{N}(0,1)
be a normal random variable with mean 0 and variance 1 that is independent of
D
and
g:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
be an arbitrary function. For every
\alpha\in(0,1)
,
Z^{2}+g(n_{A}^{1})
has a unique quantile
q_{n}^{1-\alpha}
. In particular,
\mathbb{P}\left(Z^{2}+g(n_{A}^{1})\leq q_{n}^{1-\alpha}\right)=1-\alpha
.

Proof.

Define
n_{A}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}
. The random variable
n_{A}^{1}
has finite support
\{0,\ldots,n_{A}\}
. Therefore,
Z^{2}+g(n_{A}^{1})
has the following density

f_{Z^{2}+g(n_{A}^{1})}\left(t\right)=\sum_{k=0}^{n_{A}}f_{\chi^{2}_{1}}\left(t-g\left(k\right)\right)p_{n_{A}^{1}}(k)\mathds{1}\left\{t\geq g\left(k\right)\right\},

(102)

where
p_{n_{A}^{1}}
is the probability mass function of
n_{A}^{1}
. It can be easily seen that the density function is strictly positve on its support. Hence the quantile is unique.
∎

Lemma A.15
.

Let
q_{n}^{1-\alpha}
be the
(1-\alpha)
-th quantile of
Z^{2}+g(n_{A}^{1})
, where
Z\sim\textnormal{N}(0,1)
is independent of
n_{A}^{1}
. Assume that there exist positive constants
C_{1}
,
C_{2}
and
\gamma
, such that

\inf_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\inf_{\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}}\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-C_{1}<g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)<q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-C_{2}\right)\geq\gamma.

(103)

For any
\eta\in(0,C_{2}]
, the following inequalities hold for all
\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}
and all
n\in\mathbb{N}
:

\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(Z^{2}+g(n_{A}^{1})\geq q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-\eta\right)-\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(Z^{2}+g(n_{A}^{1})\geq q_{n}^{1-\alpha}\right)\geq\eta\gamma f_{\chi_{1}^{2}}\left(C_{1}\right)

\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(Z^{2}+g(n_{A}^{1})\geq q_{n}^{1-\alpha}\right)-\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(Z^{2}+g(n_{A}^{1})\geq q_{n}^{1-\alpha}+\eta\right)\geq\eta\gamma f_{\chi_{1}^{2}}\left(C_{1}+C_{2}\right).

Proof.

We suppress the dependence on
\theta_{n}^{w}
.
Given a
\eta\in(0,C_{2}]
, the event
\{q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-C_{1}<g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)<q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-C_{2}\}
is equivalent to the event
\{\eta\leq C_{2}<q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)<C_{1}\}
. Then,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(Z^{2}+g(n_{A}^{1})\geq q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-\eta\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(Z^{2}+g(n_{A}^{1})\geq q_{n}^{1-\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\textnormal{E}\left[\mathbb{P}\left(\left.q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-\eta-g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\leq Z^{2}<q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-g(n_{A}^{1})\right|n_{A}^{1}\right)\mathds{1}\{C_{2}<q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)<C_{1}\}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\textnormal{E}\left[\int_{q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-\eta-g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)}^{q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-g(n_{A}^{1})}f_{\chi_{1}^{2}}\left(t\right)dt\mathds{1}\{C_{2}<q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-g(n_{A}^{1})<C_{1}\}\right]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\eta f_{\chi_{1}^{2}}\left(C_{1}\right)P\left(C_{2}<q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-g(n_{A}^{1})<C_{1}\right)\geq\eta\gamma f_{\chi_{1}^{2}}\left(C_{1}\right),

where the last inequality follows from (
103
), the fact that on the event
C_{2}<q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-g(n_{A}^{1})<C_{1}
we have
q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-g(n_{A}^{1})-\eta\geq 0
, and the fact the
f_{\chi_{1}^{2}}
is a decreasing function.
Similarly,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(Z^{2}+g(n_{A}^{1})\geq q_{n}^{1-\alpha}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(Z^{2}+g(n_{A}^{1})\geq q_{n}^{1-\alpha}+\eta\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\textnormal{E}\left[\mathbb{P}\left(\left.q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\leq Z^{2}<q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-g(n_{A}^{1})+\eta\right|n_{A}^{1}\right)\mathds{1}\{C_{2}<q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-g(n_{A}^{1})<C_{1}\}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\textnormal{E}\left[\int_{q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)}^{q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-g(n_{A}^{1})+\eta}f_{\chi_{1}^{2}}\left(t\right)dt\mathds{1}\{C_{2}<q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-g(n_{A}^{1})<C_{1}\}\right]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\eta f_{\chi_{1}^{2}}\left(C_{1}+\eta\right)\mathbb{P}\left(C_{2}<q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-g(n_{A}^{1})<C_{1}\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\eta f_{\chi_{1}^{2}}\left(C_{1}+C_{2}\right)\mathbb{P}\left(C_{2}<q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-g(n_{A}^{1})<C_{1}\right)\geq\eta\gamma f_{\chi_{1}^{2}}\left(C_{1}+C_{2}\right).

∎

We verify equation (
103
) for the
g_{0},g_{1}
and
g_{2}
functions defined in (
96
) and (
97
).

Lemma A.16
.

Let
g:\mathbb{R}\to[0,\infty)
be a non-negative function. For an
\alpha\in(0,1)
, let
q_{n}^{1-\alpha}
be the
\left(1-\alpha\right)
-th quantile of
Z^{2}+g(n_{A}^{1})
, where
Z\sim\textnormal{N}(0,1)
is independent of
n_{A}^{1}
. We have

q_{n}^{1-\alpha}\geq\chi^{2}_{1-\alpha},

for all
\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

Proof.

Note that for all
\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(Z^{2}+g(n_{A}^{1})\leq\chi^{2}_{1-\alpha}\right)\leq\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(Z^{2}\leq\chi^{2}_{1-\alpha}\right)=1-\alpha.

The result then follows immediately.
∎

The 75-percentile of a Chi-squared random variable with one degree of freedom is 1.32. Hence
\chi^{2}_{1-\alpha}\geq 1.32
for
\alpha\in(0,0.25]
.

Lemma A.17
.

For
\alpha\in(0,0.25]
, the
g_{0},g_{1}
and
g_{2}
functions defined in (
96
) and (
97
) satisfy (
103
) with constants

C_{1}=\chi^{2}_{1-\alpha/2}+\frac{2}{\alpha}+1,C_{2}=0.1,\gamma=\frac{0.22}{1.22}.

Proof.

We use
q_{i,n}^{1-\alpha}
to denote the quantile associated with
Z^{2}+g_{i}(n_{A}^{1})
for
i\in\{0,1,2\}
, where
Z\sim\textnormal{N}(0,1)
is independent of
n_{A}^{1}
.
Following the calculations in the proof of Theorem
A.12
, we have

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}}\textnormal{E}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left[\left|g_{i}\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\right|\right]\leq 1,i\in\{0,1,2\}.

Hence by Lemma
A.13
,
\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}}q_{i,n}^{1-\alpha}\leq\chi^{2}_{1-\alpha/2}+2/\alpha
. Let
C_{1}=\chi^{2}_{1-\alpha/2}+2/\alpha+1
and
C_{2}=0.1
. Notice
q_{i,n}^{1-\alpha}-C_{1}<0
for all
i\in\{0,1,2\}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

For
g_{0}
, we have
q_{0,n}^{1-\alpha}=\chi^{2}_{1-\alpha}
,

\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(q_{0,n}^{1-\alpha}-C_{1}<g_{0}<q_{0,n}^{1-\alpha}-C_{2}\right)\geq\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(0\leq g_{0}<\chi^{2}_{1-\alpha}-0.1\right)=1,

for all
\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}
and all
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
For
g_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(q_{1,n}^{1-\alpha}-C_{1}<g_{1}<q_{1,n}^{1-\alpha}-C_{2}\right)\geq\mathbb{P}\left(0\leq g_{1}<\chi^{2}_{1-\alpha}-C_{2}\right)

(104)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-\mathbb{P}\left(g_{1}\geq\chi^{2}_{1-\alpha}-C_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-\mathbb{P}\left(\left(\frac{n_{A}^{1}}{n_{1}}-\frac{n_{A}^{0}}{n_{0}}\right)^{2}\geq\left(\chi^{2}_{1-\alpha}-C_{2}\right)\textrm{V}_{n}\left(A\right)\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 1-\frac{\textrm{V}_{n}\left(A\right)}{\textrm{V}_{n}\left(A\right)\left(\chi^{2}_{1-\alpha}-C_{2}\right)}=1-\frac{1}{\chi^{2}_{1-\alpha}-0.1}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 1-\frac{1}{1.32-0.1}=1-\frac{1}{1.22}=\frac{0.22}{1.22},

(105)

where for (
104
) we use Lemma
A.16
and for (
105
) we use the fact that
\chi^{2}_{1-\alpha}\geq 1.32
for
\alpha\in(0,0.25]
. A similar argument holds for
g_{2}
.

∎

A.4.3
Asymptotically Valid Inference with Randomization-Based Critical Values

We state a high-level theorem establishing asymptotically valid inference using a estimator of the quantile.

Theorem A.18
.

Let
g:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
be a function such that

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}}\textnormal{E}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left[\left|g(n_{A}^{1})\right|\right]\leq C<\infty,

for a positive constant
C
. For an
\alpha\in(0,1)
, let
q_{n}^{1-\alpha}
be the
\left(1-\alpha\right)
-th quantile of the random variable
Z^{2}+g(n_{A}^{1})
, where
Z\sim\textnormal{N}(0,1)
is independent of
n_{A}^{1}
. Let
\widehat{q}_{n}
be a quantile estimator such that for each
\eta>0
we have

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sup_{\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}}\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\left|\widehat{q}_{n}-q_{n}^{1-\alpha}\right|\geq\eta\right)=0,

Given a
\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}
, let
\{\left(y_{i}(1),y_{i}(0)\right)\}_{i=1}^{n}
be the associated potential outcomes and
\{A_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
be the associated always-reporter indicators. Define
\mathcal{A}=\{i\in[n]:A_{i}=1\}
and
n_{A}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}
,
\tau_{n}=n_{A}^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\left(y_{i}(1)-y_{i}(0)\right)
. Define the variance estimator
\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}
as in (
15
) and the Hajek estimator
\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}
as in (
14
).
Then,

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sup_{\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}}\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{w}_{n}}\left(\frac{\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}-\tau_{n}\right)^{2}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}}+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq\widehat{q}_{n}\right)\leq\alpha.

(106)

Proof.

We suppress the dependence on
\theta_{n}^{w}
. By Corollary
A.11
and for any
\epsilon\in(0,2]
and
\eta>0
, we have the calculations,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}-\tau_{n}\right)^{2}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}}+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq\widehat{q}_{n}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}-\tau_{n}\right)^{2}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}}+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq\widehat{q}_{n},\left|\widehat{q}_{n}-q_{n}^{1-\alpha}\right|\leq\eta\right)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}-\tau_{n}\right)^{2}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}}+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq\widehat{q}_{n},\left|\widehat{q}_{n}-q_{n}^{1-\alpha}\right|>\eta\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}-\tau_{n}\right)^{2}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}}+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-\eta\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\widehat{q}_{n}-q_{n}^{1-\alpha}\right|\geq\eta\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(Z^{2}+g(n_{A}^{1})\geq q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-\eta\right)+C_{\ref{constant:1}}(r,s,\epsilon,B,\delta)n^{-\frac{1}{4}}+

(107)

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{\epsilon\left(\left|q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-\eta\right|+C\right)}{2\pi\left(1+\epsilon\right)}}+\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\widehat{q}_{n}-q_{n}^{1-\alpha}\right|\geq\eta\right)

(108)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(Z^{2}+g(n_{A}^{1})\geq q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-\eta\right)+C_{\ref{constant:1}}(r,s,\epsilon,B,\delta)n^{-\frac{1}{4}}

\displaystyle+\sqrt{\frac{\epsilon}{2\pi\left(1+\epsilon\right)}}\sqrt{\chi^{2}_{1-\alpha/2}+\frac{2C}{\alpha}+C+\eta}+\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\widehat{q}_{n}-q_{n}^{1-\alpha}\right|\geq\eta\right)

(109)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(Z^{2}+g(n_{A}^{1})\geq q_{n}^{1-\alpha}\right)+C_{\ref{constant:1}}(r,s,\epsilon,B,\delta)n^{-\frac{1}{4}}

\displaystyle+\sqrt{\frac{\epsilon}{2\pi\left(1+\epsilon\right)}}\sqrt{\chi^{2}_{1-\alpha/2}+\frac{2C}{\alpha}+C+\eta}+\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\widehat{q}_{n}-q_{n}^{1-\alpha}\right|\geq\eta\right)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}\left(Z^{2}+g(n_{A}^{1})\geq q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-\eta\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(Z^{2}+g(n_{A}^{1})\geq q_{n}^{1-\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\alpha+C_{\ref{constant:1}}(r,s,\epsilon,B,\delta)n^{-\frac{1}{4}}+\sqrt{\frac{\epsilon}{2\pi\left(1+\epsilon\right)}}\sqrt{\chi^{2}_{1-\alpha/2}+\frac{2C}{\alpha}+C+\eta}

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\widehat{q}_{n}-q_{n}^{1-\alpha}\right|\geq\eta\right)+\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sqrt{\eta},

(110)

where we use the estimate in (
95
) for (
107
) and (
108
), Lemma
A.13
for (
109
), and (
132
) for (
A.4.3
), which follows from the calculation

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(Z^{2}+g(n_{A}^{1})\geq q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-\eta\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(Z^{2}+g(n_{A}^{1})\geq q_{n}^{1-\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\textnormal{E}\left[\mathbb{P}\left(\left.q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-g(n_{A}^{1})-\eta\leq Z^{2}\leq q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-g(n_{A}^{1})\right|n_{A}^{1}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\textnormal{E}\left[\mathbb{P}\left(\left.q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-g(n_{A}^{1})-\eta\leq Z^{2}\leq q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-g(n_{A}^{1})\right|n_{A}^{1}\right)1\{q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-g(n_{A}^{1})-\eta\leq 0\}\right]

\displaystyle+\textnormal{E}\left[\mathbb{P}\left(\left.q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-g(n_{A}^{1})-\eta\leq Z^{2}\leq q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-g(n_{A}^{1})\right|n_{A}^{1}\right)1\{q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-g(n_{A}^{1})-\eta>0\}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\textnormal{E}\left[\mathbb{P}\left(\left.0\leq Z^{2}\leq\eta\right|n_{A}^{1}\right)1\{q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-g(n_{A}^{1})-\eta\leq 0\}\right]

\displaystyle+\textnormal{E}\left[\mathbb{P}\left(\left.q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-g(n_{A}^{1})-\eta\leq Z^{2}\leq q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-g(n_{A}^{1})\right|n_{A}^{1}\right)1\{q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-g(n_{A}^{1})-\eta>0\}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sqrt{\eta},

Taking
\limsup_{n\to\infty}
on both sides yields,

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sup_{\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}}\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\frac{\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}-\tau_{n}\right)^{2}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}}+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq\widehat{q}_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\alpha+\sqrt{\frac{\epsilon}{2\pi\left(1+\epsilon\right)}}\sqrt{\chi^{2}_{1-\alpha/2}+\frac{2C}{\alpha}+C+\eta}+\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sqrt{\eta}.

We note that the LHS of the inequality above does not depend on
\epsilon
or
\eta
. Taking
\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}
and
\lim_{\eta\to 0}
gives the desired results.
∎

Hence all we need to show is that, for all
\eta>0
,

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sup_{\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}}\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\left|\widehat{q}_{n}-q_{n}^{1-\alpha}\right|\geq\eta\right)=0.

Lemma A.19
.

Let
g
be a non-negative function such that

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}}\textnormal{E}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left[\left|g(n_{A}^{1})\right|\right]\leq C<\infty,

for a positive constant
C
and satisfies the condition (
103
) in Lemma
A.15
with positive constants
\gamma
,
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
. Let
q_{n}^{1-\alpha}
be the
\left(1-\alpha\right)
-th quantile of
Z^{2}+g(n_{A}^{1})
, where
Z\sim\textnormal{N}(0,1)
is independent of
n_{A}^{1}
.

Let
\{\widehat{q}^{1-\alpha}_{n}\}_{n}^{\infty}
be the
1-\alpha
quantile of the randomization distribution

\widehat{q}^{1-\alpha}_{n}=\inf\left\{t\in\mathbb{R}:\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\left.\left(Z^{*}\right)^{2}+g\left(n_{A}^{1*}\right)\leq t\right|D^{\textnormal{obs}}\right)\geq 1-\alpha\right\},

where
Z^{*}
and
n_{A}^{1*}
are defined in (
98
) and (
99
), and the randomization distribution is defined in (
100
).

For a given
\eta>0
, define the interval,

I_{\eta}=\left[\chi^{2}_{1-\alpha}-\eta,\chi^{2}_{1-\alpha/2}+\frac{2C}{\alpha}+\eta\right].

(111)

On the event,

\left|\widehat{q}^{1-\alpha}_{n}-q_{n}^{1-\alpha}\right|>\eta,

(112)

we must have the event
\mathcal{E}_{n}
:

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in I_{\eta}}\left|\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(Z^{2}+g(n_{A}^{1})\leq t\right)-\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\left.\left(Z^{*}\right)^{2}+g\left(n_{A}^{1*}\right)\leq t\right|D^{\textnormal{obs}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\min\{\eta\gamma f_{\chi_{1}^{2}}\left(C_{1}\right),\eta\gamma f_{\chi_{1}^{2}}\left(C_{1}+C_{2}\right)\}

In particular, if

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sup_{\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}}\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\left|\widehat{q}^{1-\alpha}_{n}-q_{n}^{1-\alpha}\right|>\eta\right)>\epsilon,

then

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sup_{\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}}\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\mathcal{E}_{n}\right)>\epsilon.

Proof.

We suppress the dependence on
\theta_{n}^{w}
for simplicity. On the event
\left|\widehat{q}^{1-\alpha}_{n}-q_{n}^{1-\alpha}\right|>\eta
, we have either
\widehat{q}^{1-\alpha}_{n}-q_{n}^{1-\alpha}>\eta
or
\widehat{q}^{1-\alpha}_{n}-q_{n}^{1-\alpha}<-\eta
.

For the case
\widehat{q}^{1-\alpha}_{n}-q_{n}^{1-\alpha}>\eta
, we have,
\widehat{q}^{1-\alpha}_{n}>q_{n}^{1-\alpha}+\eta>q_{n}^{1-\alpha}
. By the definition of
\widehat{q}^{1-\alpha}_{n}
, we have

\mathbb{P}\left(\left.\left(Z^{*}\right)^{2}+g\left(n_{A}^{1*}\right)\leq q_{n}^{1-\alpha}+\eta\right|D^{\textnormal{obs}}\right)\leq 1-\alpha

Lemma
A.15
gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(Z^{2}+g(n_{A}^{1})\leq q_{n}^{1-\alpha}+\eta\right)\geq\mathbb{P}\left(Z^{2}+g(n_{A}^{1})\leq q_{n}^{1-\alpha}\right)+\eta\gamma f_{\chi_{1}^{2}}\left(C_{1}+C_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-\alpha+\eta\gamma f_{\chi_{1}^{2}}\left(C_{1}+C_{2}\right),

where the second inequality is by Lemma
A.14
.

Hence,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left(Z^{2}+g(n_{A}^{1})\leq q_{n}^{1-\alpha}+\eta\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(\left.\left(Z^{*}\right)^{2}+g\left(n_{A}^{1*}\right)\leq q_{n}^{1-\alpha}+\eta\right|D^{\textnormal{obs}}\right)\\
\geq&\eta\gamma f_{\chi_{1}^{2}}\left(C_{1}+C_{2}\right)\end{split}

(113)

Alternatively, for the case
\widehat{q}^{1-\alpha}_{n}-q_{n}^{1-\alpha}<-\eta
, we have
\widehat{q}^{1-\alpha}_{n}<q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-\eta<q_{n}^{1-\alpha}
. By the definition of
\widehat{q}^{1-\alpha}_{n}
, we have

\mathbb{P}\left(\left.Z^{*}+g\left(n_{A}^{1*}\right)\leq q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-\eta\right|D^{\textnormal{obs}}\right)\geq 1-\alpha

Lemma
A.15
gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(Z^{2}+g(n_{A}^{1})\leq q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-\eta\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\left(Z^{2}+g(n_{A}^{1})\leq q_{n}^{1-\alpha}\right)-\eta\gamma f_{\chi_{1}^{2}}\left(C_{1}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(1-\alpha\right)-\eta\gamma f_{\chi_{1}^{2}}\left(C_{1}\right)

Hence,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left(Z^{2}+g(n_{A}^{1})\leq q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-\eta\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(\left.Z^{*}+g\left(n_{A}^{1*}\right)\leq q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-\eta\right|D^{\textnormal{obs}}\right)\\
\leq&-\eta\gamma f_{\chi_{1}^{2}}\left(C_{1}\right).\end{split}

(114)

Notice by Lemma
A.13
and Lemma
A.16
,
q_{n}^{1-\alpha}-\eta\in I_{\eta}
and
q_{n}^{1-\alpha}+\eta\in I_{\eta}
. Combining equations (
113
) and (
114
) yields the stated results.

∎

Given a vector of always-reporter indicators
A=\left(A_{i}\right)_{i=1}^{n}
. Define
\mathcal{A}=\{i\in[n]:A_{i}=1\}
and
n_{A}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}
. Given realized outcomes, we define

\overline{Y}_{A}=\frac{1}{n_{A}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A}}Y_{i},v_{A}^{*}=\frac{1}{n_{A}-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\left(Y_{i}-\overline{Y}_{A}\right)^{2}.

(115)

As (
65
), we define the target variance conditioning on the realized outcomes, with
v^{1*}_{A}=v^{0*}_{A}=v_{A}^{*}
,

\operatorname{V}_{n,k}^{*}=\frac{1}{k}v^{1*}_{A}+\frac{1}{n_{A}-k}v^{0*}_{A},

for
k\in[1,n_{A}-1]
. We note that if
\operatorname{V}^{*}_{n,k}=0
for some
k
, then all
Y_{i},i\in\mathcal{A}
are identical. Hence
\operatorname{V}^{*}_{n,k}=0
for all
k\in[1,n_{A}-1]
, and
\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}*}
and
\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}*}_{n}
are 0 conditionally almost surely. We shall define
0/0\equiv 0
when such event happens.

Theorem A.20
.

Let
g
be a non-negative function such that

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}}\textnormal{E}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left[\left|g(n_{A}^{1})\right|\right]\leq C<\infty,

for a positive constant
C
. Given a
\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}
, let
\{A_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
be the associated always-reporter indicators and define
\mathcal{A}=\{i\in[n]:A_{i}=1\}
. Suppose
n_{A}\geq 2
. For each
t\geq 0
and
\epsilon\in(0,1)
, we have,

\displaystyle\left|\left(\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\left.\left(Z^{*}\right)^{2}+g\left(n_{A}^{1*}\right)\leq t\right|D^{\textnormal{obs}}\right)\right)-\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(Z^{2}+g(n_{A}^{1})\leq t\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2(1-r)}{rs^{2}(n-1)}+\sqrt{\frac{2}{r}}C_{\ref{eqn:53}}(\delta,r)\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{A}}}\frac{\max_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\left|Y_{i}-\overline{Y}_{A}\right|}{\sqrt{v_{A}^{*}}}

Proof.

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left.\frac{\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}*}\right)^{2}}{\operatorname{V}_{n,n_{A}^{1*}}^{*}}\frac{\operatorname{V}_{n,n_{A}^{1*}}^{*}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}*}_{n}}+g\left(n_{A}^{1*}\right)\leq t\right|D^{\textnormal{obs}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left.\frac{\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}*}\right)^{2}}{\operatorname{V}_{n,n_{A}^{1*}}^{*}}\frac{\operatorname{V}_{n,n_{A}^{1*}}^{*}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}*}_{n}}+g\left(n_{A}^{1*}\right)\leq t,\frac{\operatorname{V}_{n,n_{A}^{1*}}^{*}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}*}_{n}}\geq 1-\epsilon\right|D^{\textnormal{obs}}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}\left(\left.\frac{\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}*}\right)^{2}}{\operatorname{V}_{n,n_{A}^{1*}}^{*}}\frac{\operatorname{V}_{n,n_{A}^{1*}}^{*}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}*}_{n}}+g\left(n_{A}^{1*}\right)\leq t,\frac{\operatorname{V}_{n,n_{A}^{1*}}^{*}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}*}_{n}}<1-\epsilon\right|D^{\textnormal{obs}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left.\frac{\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}*}\right)^{2}}{\operatorname{V}_{n,n_{A}^{1*}}^{*}}\left(1-\epsilon\right)+g\left(n_{A}^{1*}\right)\leq t\right|D^{\textnormal{obs}}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\left.\frac{\operatorname{V}_{n,n_{A}^{1*}}^{*}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}*}_{n}}<1-\epsilon\right|D^{\textnormal{obs}}\right)

Similarly,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left.\frac{\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}*}\right)^{2}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}*}_{n}}+g\left(n_{A}^{1*}\right)>t\right|D^{\textnormal{obs}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left.\frac{\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}*}\right)^{2}}{\operatorname{V}_{n,n_{A}^{1*}}^{*}}\left(1+\epsilon\right)+g\left(n_{A}^{1*}\right)>t\right|D^{\textnormal{obs}}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\left.\frac{\operatorname{V}_{n,n_{A}^{1*}}^{*}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}*}_{n}}>1+\epsilon\right|D^{\textnormal{obs}}\right)

Hence,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left.\frac{\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}*}\right)^{2}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}*}_{n}}+g\left(n_{A}^{1*}\right)\leq t\right|D^{\textnormal{obs}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left.\frac{\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}*}\right)^{2}}{\operatorname{V}_{n,n_{A}^{1*}}^{*}}\left(1+\epsilon\right)+g\left(n_{A}^{1*}\right)\leq t\right|D^{\textnormal{obs}}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(\left.\frac{\operatorname{V}_{n,n_{A}^{1*}}^{*}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}*}_{n}}>1+\epsilon\right|D^{\textnormal{obs}}\right)

We have the inequalities:

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left.\frac{\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}*}\right)^{2}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}*}_{n}}\left(1+\epsilon\right)+g\left(n_{A}^{1*}\right)\leq t\right|D^{\textnormal{obs}}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(Z^{2}\left(1+\epsilon\right)+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\leq t\right)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}\left(Z^{2}\left(1+\epsilon\right)+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\leq t\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(Z^{2}+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\leq t\right)

\displaystyle-\mathbb{P}\left(\left.\frac{\operatorname{V}_{n,n_{A}^{1*}}^{*}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}*}_{n}}>1+\epsilon\right|D^{\textnormal{obs}}\right),

and,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left.\frac{\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}*}\right)^{2}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}*}_{n}}+g\left(n_{A}^{1*}\right)\leq t\right|D^{\textnormal{obs}}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(Z^{2}+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\leq t\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}\left(Z^{2}\left(1-\epsilon\right)+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\leq t\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(Z^{2}+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\leq t\right)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}\left(\left.\frac{\operatorname{V}_{n,n_{A}^{1*}}^{*}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}*}_{n}}<1-\epsilon\right|D^{\textnormal{obs}}\right)

Note we have
x_{1}\leq x\leq x_{2}
implies
|x|\leq\max\{|x_{1}|,|x_{2}|\}
. Hence

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\left.\frac{\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}*}\right)^{2}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}*}_{n}}+g\left(n_{A}^{1*}\right)\leq t\right|D^{\textnormal{obs}}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(Z^{2}+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\leq t\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{s\geq 0,t}\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\left.\frac{s\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}*}\right)^{2}}{\operatorname{V}_{n,n_{A}^{1*}}^{*}}+g\left(n_{A}^{1*}\right)\leq t\right|D^{\textnormal{obs}}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(sZ^{2}+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\leq t\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

(116)

(117)

We collect the several algebraic identities in the lemma below.

Lemma A.21
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be the set of always-reporters and
D=\left(D_{i}\right)_{i=1}^{n}
be the observed assignments. Define
\overline{Y}_{A}
as in (
115
) and
\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{1}
and
\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{0}
as in (
16
). Denote
n_{A}^{1}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}
and
n_{A}^{0}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(1-D_{i}\right)A_{i}
. We have the following algebraic identities:

(i)

\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{1}-\overline{Y}_{A}=n_{A}^{-1}n_{A}^{0}\left(\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{1}-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{0}\right)
,
\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{0}-\overline{Y}_{A}=-n_{A}^{-1}n_{A}^{1}\left(\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{1}-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{0}\right)
.

(ii)

\left|Y_{i}-\overline{Y}_{A}\right|\leq\left|y_{i}(1)-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right|+\left|\overline{y}_{A}(1)-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{1}\right|+n_{A}^{-1}n_{A}^{0}\left|\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{1}-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{0}\right|
for all
i\in\mathcal{A}
if
D_{i}=1
.

(iii)

\left|Y_{i}-\overline{Y}_{A}\right|\leq\left|y_{i}(0)-\overline{y}_{A}(0)\right|+\left|\overline{y}_{A}(0)-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{0}\right|+n_{A}^{-1}n_{A}^{1}\left|\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{1}-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{0}\right|
for all
i\in\mathcal{A}
if
D_{i}=0
.

(iv)

\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\left(Y_{i}-\overline{Y}_{A}\right)^{2}=\sum_{a\in\{0,1\}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A},D_{i}=a}\left(y_{i}(a)-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{a}\right)^{2}+n_{A}^{-1}n_{A}^{1}n_{A}^{0}\left(\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{1}-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{0}\right)^{2}.

Proof.

By definition we have

\overline{Y}_{A}=\frac{n_{A}^{1}}{n_{A}}\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{1}+\frac{n_{A}^{0}}{n_{A}}\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{0}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{1}-\overline{Y}_{A}=\frac{n_{A}^{0}}{n_{A}}\left(\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{1}-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{0}\right),\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{0}-\overline{Y}_{A}=-\frac{n_{A}^{1}}{n_{A}}\left(\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{1}-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{0}\right).

This proves (i). Suppose
D_{i}=1
,

\displaystyle\left|Y_{i}-\overline{Y}_{A}\right|=\left|y_{i}(1)-\left(\frac{n_{A}^{1}}{n_{A}}\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{1}+\frac{n_{A}^{0}}{n_{A}}\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{0}\right)\right|=\left|y_{i}(1)-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{1}+\frac{n_{A}^{0}}{n_{A}}\left(\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{1}-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{0}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|y_{i}(1)-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right|+\left|\overline{y}_{A}(1)-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{1}\right|+\frac{n_{A}^{0}}{n_{A}}\left|\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{1}-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{0}\right|.

This proves (ii) and, by symmetry, (iii). For (iv), we have,

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\left(Y_{i}-\overline{Y}_{A}\right)^{2}=

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in\{0,1\}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A},D_{i}=a}\left(Y_{i}-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{a}+\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{a}-\overline{Y}_{A}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in\{0,1\}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A},D_{i}=a}\left(y_{i}(a)-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{a}\right)^{2}+\sum_{a\in\{0,1\}}n_{A}^{a}\left(\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{a}-\overline{Y}_{A}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in\{0,1\}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A},D_{i}=a}\left(y_{i}(a)-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{a}\right)^{2}+\frac{n_{A}^{1}n_{A}^{0}}{n_{A}}\left(\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{1}-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{0}\right)^{2},

where the third equality uses (i).
∎

Lemma A.22
.

Given a
\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}
, let
\{A_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
be the associated always-reporter indicators and define
\mathcal{A}=\{i\in[n]:A_{i}=1\}
and
n_{A}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}
. Given the observed outcomes
Y=\left(Y_{i}\right)_{i=1}^{n}
and define
\overline{Y}_{A}
and
v^{*}_{A}
as in (
115
).
For each
\eta>0

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sup_{\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}}\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\frac{1}{n_{A}}\frac{\max_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\left(Y_{i}-\overline{Y}_{A}\right)^{2}}{v^{*}_{A}}\geq\eta\right)\to 0

(119)

Proof.

Conditional on
D_{i}=1
and using the inequality
\left(a+b+c\right)^{2}\leq 3a^{2}+3b^{2}+3c^{2}
, we have the inequality

\displaystyle\frac{\left|Y_{i}-\overline{Y}_{A}\right|^{2}}{v_{A}^{*}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{3\left(y_{i}(1)-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)^{2}+3\left(\overline{y}_{A}(1)-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{1}\right)^{2}+3n_{A}^{-2}\left(n_{A}^{0}\right)^{2}\left(\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{1}-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{0}\right)^{2}}{\left(n_{A}-1\right)^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\left(Y_{i}-\overline{Y}_{A}\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{3\left(y_{i}(1)-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)^{2}+3\left(\overline{y}_{A}(1)-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{1}\right)^{2}+3n_{A}^{-2}\left(n_{A}^{0}\right)^{2}\left(\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{1}-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{0}\right)^{2}}{n_{A}^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\left(Y_{i}-\overline{Y}_{A}\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3\left(y_{i}(1)-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)^{2}+3\left(\overline{y}_{A}(1)-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{1}\right)^{2}+3n_{A}^{-2}\left(n_{A}^{0}\right)^{2}\left(\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{1}-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{0}\right)^{2}}{n_{A}^{-1}\left(\sum_{a\in\{0,1\}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A},D_{i}=a}\left(y_{i}(a)-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{a}\right)^{2}+n_{A}^{-1}n_{A}^{1}n_{A}^{0}\left(\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{1}-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{0}\right)^{2}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{3\left(y_{i}(1)-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)^{2}}{n_{A}^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A},D_{i}=1}\left(y_{i}(1)-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{1}\right)^{2}}+\frac{3\left(\overline{y}_{A}(1)-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{1}\right)^{2}}{n_{A}^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A},D_{i}=1}\left(y_{i}(1)-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{1}\right)^{2}}+\frac{3n^{0}_{A}}{n_{A}^{1}n_{A}}.

\frac{3n_{A}^{0}}{n_{A}n_{A}^{1}}

A similar identity holds when
D_{i}=0
. Hence we have,

\displaystyle\frac{\max_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\left(Y_{i}-\overline{Y}_{A}\right)^{2}}{v^{*}_{A}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{3\max_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\left(y_{i}(1)-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)^{2}}{n_{A}^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A},D_{i}=1}\left(y_{i}(1)-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{1}\right)^{2}}+\frac{3\max_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\left(y_{i}(0)-\overline{y}_{A}(0)\right)^{2}}{n_{A}^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A},D_{i}=0}\left(y_{i}(0)-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{0}\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{3\left(\overline{y}_{A}(1)-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{1}\right)^{2}}{n_{A}^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A},D_{i}=1}\left(y_{i}(1)-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{1}\right)^{2}}+\frac{3\left(\overline{y}_{A}(0)-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{0}\right)^{2}}{n_{A}^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A},D_{i}=0}\left(y_{i}(0)-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{0}\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{3n_{A}^{1}}{n_{A}n_{A}^{0}}+\frac{3n_{A}^{0}}{n_{A}n_{A}^{1}}.

By a union bound, we have, for each
\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\frac{1}{n_{A}}\frac{\max_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\left(Y_{i}-\overline{Y}_{A}\right)^{2}}{v^{*}_{A}}\geq\eta\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in\{0,1\}}\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\frac{1}{n_{A}}\frac{3\max_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\left(y_{i}(a)-\overline{y}_{A}(a)\right)^{2}}{n_{A}^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A},D_{i}=a}\left(y_{i}(a)-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{a}\right)^{2}}\geq\frac{\eta}{6}\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{a\in\{0,1\}}\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\frac{1}{n_{A}}\frac{3\left(\overline{y}_{A}(a)-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{a}\right)^{2}}{n_{A}^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A},D_{i}=a}\left(y_{i}(a)-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{a}\right)^{2}}\geq\frac{\eta}{6}\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{a\in\{0,1\}}\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\frac{1}{n_{A}}\frac{3n^{1-a}_{A}}{n_{A}^{a}n_{A}}\geq\frac{\eta}{6}\right)

By Lemma
A.23
below,

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sup_{\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}}\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\frac{1}{n_{A}}\frac{\max_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\left(Y_{i}-\overline{Y}_{A}\right)^{2}}{v^{*}_{A}}\geq\eta\right)=0

∎

Lemma A.23
.

Given the setup in Lemma
A.21
and Lemma
A.22
, for every
\eta>0
and
a\in\{0,1\}
, following statements hold

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sup_{\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}}\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\frac{1}{n_{A}}\frac{3\max_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\left(y_{i}(a)-\overline{y}_{A}(a)\right)^{2}}{n_{A}^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A},D_{i}=a}\left(y_{i}(a)-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{a}\right)^{2}}\geq\eta\right)\to 0,

(120)

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sup_{\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}}\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\frac{1}{n_{A}}\frac{3\left(\overline{y}_{A}(a)-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{a}\right)^{2}}{n_{A}^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A},D_{i}=a}\left(y_{i}(a)-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{a}\right)^{2}}\geq\eta\right)\to 0,

(121)

and

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sup_{\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}}\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\frac{n^{1-a}_{A}}{n_{A}^{a}n^{2}_{A}}\geq\eta\right)\to 0.

(122)

Proof.

We prove the case for
a=1
. The case for
a=0
can be proved analogously. Rearranging the left-hand side in (
120
), we have,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{A}}\frac{3\max_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\left(y_{i}(1)-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)^{2}}{n_{A}^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A},D_{i}=1}\left(y_{i}(1)-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{1}\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{A}}\frac{3\max_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\left(y_{i}(1)-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)^{2}}{v_{A}^{1}}\times\frac{v^{1}_{A}}{n_{A}^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A},D_{i}=1}\left(y_{i}(1)-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{1}\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{3B^{2}}{\sqrt{n_{A}}}\times\frac{n_{A}}{n_{A}^{1}}\times\frac{v^{1}_{A}}{\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A},D_{i}=1}\left(y_{i}(1)-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{1}\right)^{2}},

where the last inequality is by Lemma
A.6
.
We prove

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sup_{\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}}\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\frac{B^{2}}{\sqrt{n_{A}}}\times\frac{n_{A}}{n_{A}^{1}}\times\frac{v^{1}_{A}}{\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A},D_{i}=1}\left(y_{i}(1)-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{1}\right)^{2}}\geq\eta\right)\to 0.

For
\epsilon
sufficiently small and applying a union bound, we prove

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sup_{\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}}\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\frac{B^{2}}{\sqrt{n_{A}}}\times\frac{n_{A}}{n_{A}^{1}}\geq\frac{\eta}{1+\epsilon}\right)\to 0.

(123)

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sup_{\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}}\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\frac{v^{1}_{A}}{\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A},D_{i}=1}\left(y_{i}(1)-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{1}\right)^{2}}\geq 1+\epsilon\right)\to 0

(124)

For (
123
), because both sides in the event are positive, we note

\displaystyle\frac{B^{2}}{\sqrt{n_{A}}}\times\frac{n_{A}}{n_{A}^{1}}\geq\frac{\eta}{1+\epsilon}\Leftrightarrow\frac{n_{A}^{1}}{n_{A}}\frac{\sqrt{n_{A}}}{B^{2}}\leq\frac{1+\epsilon}{\eta}

(125)

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle\frac{n_{A}^{1}}{n_{A}}\leq\frac{1+\epsilon}{\eta}\frac{B^{2}}{\sqrt{n_{A}}}\Rightarrow\frac{n_{A}^{1}}{n_{A}}\leq\frac{1+\epsilon}{\eta\sqrt{s}}\frac{B^{2}}{\sqrt{n}},

(126)

where the last implication is by Assumption
2
-
(i)
. Notice
\textnormal{E}\left[n^{a}_{A}\right]=n^{-1}n_{1}n_{A}
and
n_{A}^{-1}\textnormal{E}\left[n^{a}_{A}\right]=n^{-1}n_{1}=\pi\in[r,1-r]
by Assumption
3
. For
n
large enough we have,

\frac{1+\epsilon}{\eta\sqrt{s}}\frac{B^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}-\pi\leq\frac{1+\epsilon}{\eta\sqrt{s}}\frac{B^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}-r<0.

Hence we have,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\frac{B^{2}}{\sqrt{n_{A}}}\times\frac{n_{A}}{n_{A}^{1}}\geq\frac{\eta}{1+\epsilon}\right)\leq\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\frac{n_{A}^{1}}{n_{A}}\leq\frac{1+\epsilon}{\eta\sqrt{s}}\frac{B^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\frac{n_{A}^{1}}{n_{A}}-\pi\leq\frac{1+\epsilon}{\eta\sqrt{s}}\frac{B^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}-\pi\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\left(\frac{n_{A}^{1}}{n_{A}}-\pi\right)^{2}\geq\left(\frac{1+\epsilon}{\eta\sqrt{s}}\frac{B^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}-\pi\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\textnormal{E}\left[\left(\frac{n_{A}^{1}}{n_{A}}-\pi\right)^{2}\right]/\left(\frac{1+\epsilon}{\eta\sqrt{s}}\frac{B^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}-\pi\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\left(1-r\right)^{3}}{4rs^{2}}\frac{1}{n-1}/\left(\frac{1+\epsilon}{\eta\sqrt{s}}\frac{B^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}-\pi\right)^{2}=O(n^{-1}),

where the last inequality follows from Lemma
A.2
and

\displaystyle\textrm{V}\left(\frac{n^{1}_{A}}{n_{A}}\right)=\left(\frac{n_{1}}{n_{A}}\right)^{2}\textrm{V}\left(\frac{n^{1}_{A}}{n_{1}}\right)=\left(\frac{n_{1}}{n_{A}}\right)^{2}\frac{n_{0}}{n_{1}n}\frac{1}{n-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(A_{i}-\overline{A}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1-r}{s}\right)^{2}\frac{1-r}{r}\frac{1}{n-1}\frac{1}{4}=\frac{\left(1-r\right)^{3}}{4rs^{2}}\frac{1}{n-1}.

Define events
\mathcal{E}
and
\mathcal{E}^{c}
as in (
56
) and (
57
). Recall the property in (
89
) on
\mathcal{E}^{c}
. (
122
) follows from a calculation:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\frac{n^{1-a}_{A}}{n_{A}^{a}n^{2}_{A}}\geq\eta\right)\leq\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\mathcal{E}\right)+\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\frac{1-r/2}{r/2}\frac{1}{n^{2}_{A}}\geq\eta\right)\to 0

We hence omit.
For
\epsilon
small enough, (
124
) follows from the calculation in (
90
).

We now show (
121
). The event can be written as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{A}}\frac{3\left(\overline{y}_{A}(1)-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{1}\right)^{2}}{v^{1}_{A}}\frac{v^{1}_{A}}{\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A},D_{i}=1}\left(y_{i}(1)-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{1}\right)^{2}}\frac{n_{A}}{n_{A}^{1}}

(127)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\eta}{\left(1+\epsilon\right)^{2}}\left(1+\epsilon\right)^{2}

(128)

Again by a union bound, we can shown as in (
123
)

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sup_{\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}}\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{A}}}\frac{n_{A}}{n_{A}^{1}}\geq\frac{\eta}{\left(1+\epsilon\right)^{2}}\right)=0,

(129)

and as in (
124
)

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sup_{\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}}\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\frac{v^{1}_{A}}{\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A},D_{i}=1}\left(y_{i}(1)-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{1}\right)^{2}}\geq 1+\epsilon\right)=0.

We only need to show

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sup_{\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}}\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{A}}}\frac{3\left(\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{1}-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)^{2}}{v_{A}^{1}}\geq 1+\epsilon\right)=0

(130)

Recall the definition of the event
\mathcal{E}
from (
56
) and
\mathcal{E}^{c}
from (
57
)

\displaystyle\textnormal{E}\left[\mathbb{P}\left(\left.\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{A}}}\frac{3\left(\overline{y}_{A}(1)-\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{1}\right)^{2}}{v_{A}^{1}}\geq 1+\epsilon\right|n_{A}^{1}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{E}\right)+\textnormal{E}\left[\left.\mathbb{P}\left(\left.\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{A}}}\frac{3\left(\widehat{\tau}_{A}^{1}-\overline{y}_{A}(1)\right)^{2}}{v_{A}^{1}}\geq 1+\epsilon\right|n_{A}^{1}\right)\right|\mathcal{E}^{c}\right]\times\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{E}^{c}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{E}\right)+\textnormal{E}\left[\left.\frac{3}{1+\epsilon}\frac{n_{A}^{0}}{n_{A}^{1}n_{A}\sqrt{n_{A}}}\right|\mathcal{E}^{c}\right]\times\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{E}^{c}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2(1-r)}{rs^{2}(n-1)}+\frac{3}{1+\epsilon}\frac{2-r}{r}s^{-\frac{3}{2}}n^{-\frac{3}{2}}=O(n^{-1}).

∎

Theorem A.24
.

Let
g:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
be a function such that

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}}\textnormal{E}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left[\left|g(n_{A}^{1})\right|\right]\leq C<\infty,

for a positive constant
C
.

Given a
\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}
, let
\{A_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
be the associated always-reporter indicators and denote a completely randomized design
D=\left(D_{i}\right)_{i=1}^{n}\sim\mathrm{CR}(n,n_{1})
. Define
n_{A}^{1}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}
. Let
Z\sim\textrm{N}(0,1)
be a normal random variable with mean 0 and variance 1 that is independent of
D
. Recall the definition of
Z^{*}
from (
98
) and
n_{A}^{1}*
from (
99
).
For a given
\eta>0
, define the interval,

I_{\eta}=\left[\chi^{2}_{1-\alpha}-\eta,\chi^{2}_{1-\alpha/2}+\frac{2C}{\alpha}+\eta\right],

We have, for each
\mu>0
, define the event
\mathcal{E}_{n}
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{n}:\sup_{t\in I_{\eta}}\left|\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(Z^{2}+g(n_{A}^{1})\leq t\right)-\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\left.\left(Z^{*}\right)^{2}+g\left(n_{A}^{1*}\right)\leq t\right|D^{\textnormal{obs}}\right)\right|\geq\mu.

We have

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sup_{\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}}\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\mathcal{E}_{n}\right)=0

Proof.

We note that because the underlying probability space is discrete
\mathcal{E}_{n}
is measurable. Define
t_{\textrm{max}}=\left|\chi^{2}_{1-\alpha/2}+\frac{2C}{\alpha}+\eta\right|
.
By Theorem
A.20
, for sufficiently large
n
and small
\epsilon>0
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{2(1-r)}{rs^{2}(n-1)}+C_{\ref{constant:2}}(r,s,\epsilon)n^{-1}+\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\sqrt{\frac{\epsilon}{1-\epsilon}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\sqrt{\frac{\epsilon}{1+\epsilon}}\right)\sqrt{t_{\textrm{max}}+C}<\frac{\mu}{2}.

Then the result follows from Lemma
A.22
.

∎

Theorem A.25
.

Let
g
be a non-negative function such that

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}}\textnormal{E}_{\theta^{w}_{n}}\left[\left|g(n_{A}^{1})\right|\right]\leq C<\infty,

and satisfies the condition (
103
) in Lemma
A.15
with positive constants
\gamma
,
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
. Given
\alpha\in(0,1)
, let
q_{n}^{1-\alpha}
be the
\left(1-\alpha\right)
-th quantile of
Z^{2}+g(n_{A}^{1})
, where
Z\sim\textnormal{N}(0,1)
is independent of
n_{A}^{1}
.

Let
\{\widehat{q}^{1-\alpha}_{n}\}_{n}^{\infty}
be the
1-\alpha
quantile of the randomization distribution

\widehat{q}^{1-\alpha}_{n}=\inf\left\{t\in\mathbb{R}:\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\left.\left(Z^{*}\right)^{2}+g\left(n_{A}^{1*}\right)\leq t\right|D^{\textnormal{obs}}\right)\geq 1-\alpha\right\},

where
Z^{*}
and
n_{A}^{1*}
are defined in (
98
) and (
99
). For each
\eta>0
, we have

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sup_{\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}}\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\left|\widehat{q}^{1-\alpha}_{n}-q_{n}^{1-\alpha}\right|>\eta\right)=0.

Moreover,

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sup_{\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}}\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{w}_{n}}\left(\frac{\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}-\tau_{n}\right)^{2}}{\widehat{\operatorname{V}}^{\textnormal{hj}}_{n}}+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq\widehat{q}_{n}^{1-\alpha}\right)\leq\alpha

(131)

Proof.

This is proved by Theorem
A.24
, Lemma
A.19
(through a proof by contradiction), and Theorem
A.18
.
∎

A.5
Auxiliary Lemmas

We note that
Z^{2}
with
Z\sim\textnormal{N}\left(0,1\right)
follows a Chi-squared
\chi^{2}_{1}
distribution with one degree of freedom.
Z^{2}
has a density function:

f_{\chi^{2}_{1}}(t)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}t^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-\frac{t}{2}},

for
t\in\left(0,\infty\right)
. Note that
f_{\chi^{2}_{1}}(t)
is a decreasing function of
t\in\left(0,\infty\right)
. We have the following inequality:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sup_{x\geq 0,s\in[0,\delta]}\int_{x}^{x+s}f_{\chi^{2}_{1}}(t)dt=\int_{0}^{\delta}f_{\chi^{2}_{1}}(t)dt\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{0}^{\delta}t^{-\frac{1}{2}}dt=\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sqrt{\delta}.\end{split}

(132)

Lemma A.26
.

Let
Z^{2}
with
Z\sim\textnormal{N}\left(0,1\right)
be a random variable independent of
D=\left(D_{i}\right)_{i=1}^{n}\sim\textrm{CR}(n,n_{1})
, and
g:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
be a function such that

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{\theta^{w}_{n}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}}\textnormal{E}_{\theta^{w}_{n}}\left[\left|g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\right|\right]\leq C<\infty

(133)

for some positive constant
C
. For every
\epsilon\in(0,1)
and
t\in\mathbb{R}
, we have the inequalities

\left|\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(Z^{2}\left(1+\epsilon\right)+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq t\right)-\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(Z^{2}+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq t\right)\right|\leq\sqrt{\frac{2\epsilon\left(|t|+C\right)}{\pi\left(1+\epsilon\right)}},

\left|\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(Z^{2}+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq t\right)-\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(Z^{2}\left(1-\epsilon\right)+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq t\right)\right|\leq\sqrt{\frac{2\epsilon\left(|t|+C\right)}{\pi\left(1-\epsilon\right)}},

for all
\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}
and all
n\in\mathbb{N}
. In addition, we have,

Proof.

We first show the result for
\left|\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(Z^{2}+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq t\right)-\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(Z^{2}\left(1-\epsilon\right)+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq t\right)\right|
. For simplicity, we suppress the dependence on
\theta_{n}^{w}
. For every
\epsilon\in(0,1)
, we have

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(Z^{2}\left(1+\epsilon\right)+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq t\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(Z^{2}+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq t\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{t-g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)}{1+\epsilon}\leq Z^{2}<t-g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\textnormal{E}\left[\mathbb{P}\left(\left.\frac{t-g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)}{1+\epsilon}\leq Z^{2}<t-g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\right|n_{A}^{1}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\textnormal{E}\left[\mathbb{P}\left(\left.\frac{t-g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)}{1+\epsilon}\leq Z^{2}<t-g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\right|n_{A}^{1}\right)\mathds{1}\left\{t-g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq 0\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\textnormal{E}\left[\sqrt{\frac{\epsilon}{1+\epsilon}}\sqrt{t-g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)}\mathds{1}\left\{t-g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq 0\right\}\right]

(134)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sqrt{\frac{\epsilon}{1+\epsilon}}\sqrt{\textnormal{E}\left[\left|t-g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\right|\right]}

(135)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sqrt{\frac{\epsilon}{1+\epsilon}}\sqrt{|t|+\textnormal{E}\left[\left|g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\right|\right]}\leq\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sqrt{\frac{\epsilon}{1+\epsilon}}\sqrt{|t|+C},

where the positive constant
C
is defined in (
133
), (
134
) follows from (
132
), and (
135
) follows from
\textnormal{E}[X]\leq\sqrt{\textnormal{E}[X^{2}]}
for any square-integrable random variable
X
. The result for
\left|\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(Z^{2}+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq t\right)-\mathbb{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(Z^{2}\left(1-\epsilon\right)+g\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq t\right)\right|
can be shown similarly.
∎

A.6
Proof of Theorem
4.2
and Theorem
4.4

We now prove Theorem
4.2
.

Proof.

We write the underlying true table as
A^{0}
.
Under the sharp-null hypothesis,

\displaystyle\textnormal{P}_{\theta_{n}^{s}}\left(p^{\textrm{worst}}\leq\alpha\right)\leq

\displaystyle\textnormal{P}_{\theta_{n}^{s}}\left(p\left(A^{0}\right)\leq\alpha-\beta\right)+\textnormal{P}_{\theta_{n}^{s}}\left(A^{0}\not\in\mathbb{A}\left(D,R\right)\right)

(136)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\alpha-\beta+\beta=\alpha.

(137)

Similarly, under the weak-null hypothesis,

\displaystyle\textnormal{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(p^{\textrm{worst}}\leq\alpha\right)\leq

\displaystyle\textnormal{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(p\left(A^{0}\right)\leq\alpha-\beta\right)+\textnormal{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(A^{0}\not\in\mathbb{A}\left(D,R\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\textnormal{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\mathcal{T}^{0}_{n}+g_{i}\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq\widehat{q}^{i,\alpha-\beta}_{n}\right)+\beta,

for
i\in\{0,1,2\}
, where the function
g_{i}(n_{A}^{1})
is defined in (
96
) or (
97
) and
\widehat{q}^{i,1-\alpha+\beta}_{n}
is the randomization-based critical value of the corresponding function
g_{i}
.
\mathcal{T}^{0}_{n}
is defined in (
17
).

We have shown that
g_{i}(n_{A}^{1})
satisfies the premise of Theorem
A.25
in Theorem
A.12
.
Hence by Theorem
A.25
,

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sup_{\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}}\textnormal{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(p^{\textrm{worst}}\leq\alpha\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sup_{\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}}\textnormal{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\mathcal{T}_{n}^{0}+g_{i}\left(n_{A}^{i}\right)\geq\widehat{q}^{i,1-\alpha+\beta}_{n}\right)+\beta\leq\alpha-\beta+\beta=\alpha.

∎

We now prove Theorem
4.4
.

Proof.

Let
i\in\{0,1,2\}
. We write the underlying true table as
A^{0}
and define
k_{0}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}^{0}
. Algorithm
3
will reject the weak-null hypothesis if
\mathcal{T}^{\max,k}_{i}\geq q^{k}_{i,1-\alpha+\beta}
for all
k
in
Cardinality-A
. Then we have,

\displaystyle\textnormal{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\mathcal{T}^{\max,k}_{i}\geq q^{k}_{i,1-\alpha+\beta},\forall k\in\textrm{Cardinality-A}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\textnormal{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\mathcal{T}^{\max,k_{0}}_{i}\geq q^{k_{0}}_{i,1-\alpha+\beta}\right)+\textnormal{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(A^{0}\not\in\mathbb{A}\left(D,R\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\textnormal{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\mathcal{T}_{n}^{i}+g_{i}\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq q^{k_{0}}_{i,1-\alpha+\beta}\right)+\beta

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\textnormal{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(Z^{2}+g_{i}(n_{A}^{1})\geq q^{k_{0}}_{i,1-\alpha+\beta}\right)+\beta

\displaystyle+\left|\underbrace{\textnormal{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\mathcal{T}_{n}^{i}+g_{i}\left(n_{A}^{1}\right)\geq q^{k_{0}}_{i,1-\alpha+\beta}\right)-\textnormal{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(Z^{2}+g_{i}(n_{A}^{1})\geq q^{k_{0}}_{i,1-\alpha+\beta}\right)}_{(*)}\right|.

By Lemma
A.13
, Theorem
A.12
and Lemma
A.16
, the set of all possible quantiles for
q^{k}_{i,1-\alpha+\beta}
lies in a bounded set. Using an argument similar as the justification from (
108
) to (
109
), we can show that,

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sup_{\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}}\left|(*)\right|=0.

Hence we have,

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sup_{\theta_{n}^{w}\in\Theta_{n}^{w}}\textnormal{P}_{\theta_{n}^{w}}\left(\mathcal{T}^{\max,k}_{i}\geq q^{k}_{i,1-\alpha+\beta},\forall k\in\textrm{Cardinality-A}\right)\leq\alpha.

∎

Appendix B
Proof for Results in Section
5

B.1
Proof of Lemma
5.1

Proof.

Let
A^{*}
be the optimizing always-reporter vector associated with problem (
7
).
By definition, there must exist an interval
[t_{i-1},t_{i}]
such that
\mathcal{T}_{n}(Y,D^{\textnormal{obs}},A^{*})\in[t_{i-1},t_{i}]
. Let
A^{i*}
be the optimizing vector corresponding to the subproblem of (
38
) associated with
[t_{i-1},t_{i}]
. We have the inequality:

\displaystyle\textnormal{E}_{D}\left[\mathds{1}\left(\mathcal{T}_{n}(Y,D,A^{i*})\geq t_{i-1}\right)\right]\geq\textnormal{E}_{D}\left[\mathds{1}\left(\mathcal{T}_{n}(Y,D,A^{*})\geq t_{i-1}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\geq\textnormal{E}_{D}\left[\mathds{1}\left(\mathcal{T}_{n}(Y,D,A^{*})\geq\mathcal{T}_{n}(Y,D^{\textnormal{obs}},A^{*})\right)\right]=p^{\textrm{worst}},

where
D\sim\textrm{CR}(n,n_{1})
. For every interval
[t_{i-1},t_{i}]
and its associated optimized value, we have the inequality:

\displaystyle v_{i}=

\displaystyle\textnormal{E}_{D}\left[\mathds{1}\left(\mathcal{T}_{n}(Y,D,A^{i*})\geq t_{i-1}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\textnormal{E}_{D}\left[\mathds{1}\left(\mathcal{T}_{n}(Y,D,A^{i*})\geq t_{i}\right)\right]+\textnormal{E}_{D}\left[\mathds{1}\left(\mathcal{T}_{n}(Y,D,A^{i*})\in[t_{i-1},t_{i})\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\textnormal{E}_{D}\left[\mathds{1}\left(\mathcal{T}_{n}(Y,D,A^{i*})\geq\mathcal{T}_{n}(Y,D^{\textnormal{obs}},A^{i*})\right)\right]+\textnormal{E}_{D}\left[\mathds{1}\left(\mathcal{T}_{n}(Y,D,A^{i*})\in[t_{i-1},t_{i})\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle p^{\textrm{worst}}+\textnormal{E}_{D}\left[\mathds{1}\left(\mathcal{T}_{n}(Y,D,A^{i*})\in[t_{i-1},t_{i})\right)\right].

Combining the inequalities yields the desired results.
∎

Appendix C
Computational Subroutines

Section
C.1
includes two master algorithms that computer the worst-case p-values for different outcome types:

1.

A_master_continuous
: for continuous outcomes.

2.

A_master_finite_support
: for outcomes with a small support.

Section
C.2
includes subroutines that are used in
A_master_continuous
:

1.

B_Calculate_Heuristic_p_Value
: given a number of always-reporters, the subroutine finds a heuristic solution for the always-reporter vector that maximizes the p-value.

2.

B_Create_Lower_Bound:
an MIQCP-based bisection method for constructing a lower bound for the test statistic

\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}^{0}_{n}(Y,D^{A},x)+g_{i}(n_{A,1}),

subject to
x\in x(k,D^{\textnormal{obs}},R)
, for a given
k
, an assignment vector
D^{A}
, and a statistic type
i\in\{0,1,2\}
. Here,
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}^{0}_{n}(Y,D^{A},x)
is defined in (
40
), and the functions
g_{i}(n_{A,1})
are defined in (
96
) and (
97
).

3.

B_Create_Upper_Bound:
an MIQCP-based bisection method for constructing an upper bound for the same constrained problem considered in
B_Create_Lower_Bound
.

4.

B_Create_Lower_Bound_2:
a MIQCP method for constructing a lower bound of the statistic

\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{2}(Y,D^{A},x^{A})-c\times\widehat{\sigma}^{2,\textnormal{hj}}_{n}\!\left(Y,D^{A},x^{A}\right)

subject to
x\in x(k,D^{\textnormal{obs}},R)
for a given
k
, an assignment vector
D^{A}
and a given scalar
c
, where the functions
\mu^{2}_{n}
and
\widehat{\sigma}^{2,\textnormal{hj}}_{n}\!\left(Y,D^{A},x^{A}\right)
are defined below (
40
).

5.

B_Calculate_p_Value_Upper_Bound
: an algorithm that solves the subproblems described in Theorem
5.3
.

Section
C.3
includes algorithms for asymptotic inferences:

1.

A_master_asymptotics
: a master algorithm that solves the asymptotic inference problem.

2.

B_Asymptotic_Inferences_Inner
: a subroutine that is used in the master algorithm.

Section
C.4
includes

1.

C_dim2_bounds
: calculate analytical upper and lower bounds for the squared difference-in-means estimator.

2.

C_v1_min
and
C_v0_min
: calculate analytical lower bounds for the treated-group and control-group variance estimators.

3.

C_v1_max
and
C_v0_max
: calculate analytical upper bounds for the treated-group and control-group variance estimators.

We adopt the indexing convention from Section
5.3
. Recall that the first
r_{0}
units are always-reporters assigned to the control group, regardless of whether we index by
i
or by
a
. Also recall the set of matching variables associated with a fixed number of always-reporters
k\in[\,r_{0},\;r_{0}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}R_{i}\,]
, as defined in (
41
):

\begin{split}&x(k,D^{\textnormal{obs}},R)=\\
&\left\{\{x_{ai}\}_{a\in[k],i\in[n]}:\begin{array}[]{ll}x_{ai}\in\{0,1\}&\forall a\in[k],i\in[n],\\
x_{ai}=1,&\forall a=i,a\leq r_{0}\\
\sum_{i}x_{ai}=1,&\forall a\in[k],\\
\sum_{a}x_{ai}\leq 1,&\forall i\in[n],\\
x_{ai}\in\{0,1\},&\forall a\in[k],D_{i}^{\textnormal{obs}}=1,R_{i}=1,\\
x_{ai}=0,&\forall a\in[k],D_{i}^{\textnormal{obs}}=1,R_{i}=0.\end{array}\right\}.\end{split}

Recall our notation for the observed dataset
\mathcal{D}=\left(Y,D^{\textnormal{obs}},R\right)
as defined in (
3
). We define the subroutines
zeros
(
n_{1}
,
n_{2}
) and
ones
(
n_{1}
,
n_{2}
), which create a
n_{1}
-by-
n_{2}
matrix of zeros and ones, respectively.

For brevity, we assume that our programs have access to basic information about the experimental design and statistical inferential parameters, including the sample size
n
, the number of treated units
n_{1}
, the number of control units
n_{0}
, the number of always-reporters in the control group
r_{0}
, the number of simulated assignments
n_{mc}
, the significance level
\alpha
, and the statistic type
i
for testing the balance of the number of always-reporters.

Finally, recall the definition of
\widetilde{p}(x)
and
\widetilde{v}(x,t_{i-1},t_{i})
from (
43
) and (
44
). For a given number of always-reporters and due to the underlying symmetry of the assignment distribution, the optimal values of the problems

\max_{x\in x(k,D^{\textnormal{obs}},R)}\widetilde{p}(x)\text{ and }\max_{x\in x(k,D^{\textnormal{obs}},R)}\widetilde{v}(x,t_{i-1},t_{i})

(138)

are equivalent to the optimal values of the problems

\max_{x\in x(k,D^{\textnormal{obs}},R)}\widetilde{p}(x)\text{ subject to }\tilde{Y}_{a}\geq\tilde{Y}_{a+1},\forall a\in[r_{0}+1,n_{A}-1]

\max_{x\in x(k,D^{\textnormal{obs}},R)}\widetilde{v}(x,t_{i-1},t_{i})\text{ subject to }\tilde{Y}_{a}\geq\tilde{Y}_{a+1},\forall a\in[r_{0}+1,n_{A}-1],

where
\tilde{Y_{a}}=\sum_{a,i}x_{ai}Y_{i}
,
\forall a\in[n_{A}]
and note that the outcomes for the first
r_{0}
units are fixed under our convention. We include these constraints in the optimization problems below because it reduces the symmetry of the undelrying integer programming problems, possibly making it faster to solve.

Algorithm 5

A_master_small_support:
test null hypotheses at a prespecified significance level
\alpha\in(0,1)
.

To implement the step “select
A\in\mathbb{A}\!\left(D^{\textnormal{obs}},R\right)
such that
c(A)=c
” efficiently, one can group units by outcome category and then assign always-reporter indicators to match the target count vector
c
(i.e., by selecting the appropriate number of units within each outcome category).

C.2
Subroutines for
A_master_continuous

We define the following functions. Given a number of always-reporters
n_{A}
, a vector of assignments
D^{A}=\left\{D_{a}\right\}_{a\in[n_{A}]}
and outcomes
Y^{A}=\left\{Y_{a}\right\}_{a\in[n_{A}]}
.

\displaystyle\mathrm{DIM}^{2}(D^{A},Y^{A})

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\sum_{a}D_{a}Y_{a}}{\sum_{a}D_{a}}-\frac{\sum_{a}\left(1-D_{a}\right)Y_{a}}{\sum_{a}\left(1-D_{a}\right)}\right)^{2},

(139)

\displaystyle\mathrm{VAR}(D^{A},Y^{A})

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{a}D_{a}Y_{a}^{2}}{\left(\sum_{a}D_{a}\right)^{2}}-\frac{1}{\sum_{a}D_{a}}\left(\frac{\sum_{a}D_{a}Y_{a}}{\sum_{a}D_{a}}\right)^{2}

(140)

\displaystyle+\frac{\sum_{a}\left(1-D_{a}\right)Y_{a}^{2}}{\left(\sum_{a}\left(1-D_{a}\right)\right)^{2}}-\frac{1}{\sum_{a}\left(1-D_{a}\right)}\left(\frac{\sum_{a}\left(1-D_{a}\right)Y_{a}}{\sum_{a}\left(1-D_{a}\right)}\right)^{2}

(141)

\displaystyle\textrm{V}(n_{A})

\displaystyle=\frac{n^{2}}{n_{1}n_{0}(n-1)}\frac{n_{A}}{n}\left(1-\frac{n_{A}}{n}\right),

(142)

\displaystyle\textrm{N}_{A}^{0}(D^{A})

\displaystyle=0,

(143)

\displaystyle\textrm{N}_{A}^{1}(D^{A})

\displaystyle=\frac{\left(n_{1}^{-1}\sum_{a}D_{a}-n_{0}^{-1}\sum_{a}\left(1-D_{a}\right)\right)^{2}}{\textrm{V}(n_{A})},

(144)

\displaystyle\textrm{N}_{A}^{2}(D^{A})

\displaystyle=\frac{\left(\lfloor n_{1}^{-1}\sum_{a}D_{a}-n_{0}^{-1}\sum_{a}\left(1-D_{a}\right)\rfloor_{-}\right)^{2}}{\textrm{V}(n_{A})}.

(145)

For a given number of always-reporters
k
, recall the definition of the distribution of
\{D^{A}_{a}\}_{a\in[k]}\sim\mathcal{L}(n,n_{1},k)
as defined in Section
5.3
.

The function below provides a heuristic solution to the worst-case
p
-value problem for a given number of always-reporters. We note that
NUM_L
and
NUM_U
are analytical lower and upper bounds for the variance estimators, while
DIM2_L
and
DIM2_U
are analytical lower and upper bounds for the squared difference-in-means estimator. The corresponding construction routines are provided in Section
C.4
. The analytical bounds are generally looser than those obtained via computational approaches.

Algorithm 6

B_Calculate_Heuristic_p_Value:
calculate lower bounds for the worst-case p-value.

1:

Dataset
\mathcal{D}
, number of always-reporters
n_{A}
, (optional)
tol=10^{-4}
.

2:

Create assignment vector
D^{A}_{\textnormal{obs}}=[\texttt{ones}(n_{A},1),\texttt{zeros}(r_{0},1)]
.

3:

Set
N_{A}\leftarrow\mathrm{N}^{i}_{A}(D^{A}_{\textnormal{obs}})
.

4:

Set
\textrm{DIM2\_L},\textrm{DIM2\_U}\leftarrow\texttt{C\_dim2\_bounds}(D^{A}_{\textnormal{obs}},\mathcal{D})
.

5:

Set
\textrm{NUM\_L}\leftarrow\texttt{C\_v1\_min}(D^{A}_{\textnormal{obs}},\mathcal{D})+\texttt{C\_v0\_min}(D^{A}_{\textnormal{obs}},\mathcal{D})
.

6:

Set
\textrm{NUM\_U}\leftarrow\texttt{C\_v1\_max}(D^{A}_{\textnormal{obs}},\mathcal{D})+\texttt{C\_v0\_max}(D^{A}_{\textnormal{obs}},\mathcal{D})
.

7:

Set
L\leftarrow\textrm{DIM2\_L}/\textrm{NUM\_U}+N_{A}
.

8:

Set
U\leftarrow\textrm{DIM2\_U}/\textrm{NUM\_L}+N_{A}
.

9:

L,Y^{A}\leftarrow\texttt{B\_Create\_Lower\_Bound}(D^{A}_{\textnormal{obs}},n_{A},\mathcal{D},L,U,tol).

10:

Set

pval=\frac{1}{n_{mc}}\sum_{s=1}^{n_{mc}}1\left\{\mathcal{T}^{\textnormal{obs}}\left(D^{A}_{\textnormal{obs}},Y^{A}\right)\geq\mathcal{T}^{\textnormal{obs}}\left(D^{A}_{s},Y^{A}\right)\right\},

(146)

where
D^{A}_{s}\sim\mathcal{L}(n,n_{1},n_{A}),\forall s\in[n_{mc}]
,

\mathcal{T}^{\textnormal{obs}}(D^{A}_{\textnormal{obs}},Y^{A})=\frac{\mathrm{DIM}^{2}(D^{A}_{\textnormal{obs}},Y^{A})}{\mathrm{VAR}(D^{A}_{\textnormal{obs}},Y^{A})}+\mathrm{N}_{A}^{i}(D^{A}_{\textnormal{obs}}),

and,

\mathcal{T}^{\textnormal{obs}}(D^{A}_{s},Y^{A})=\frac{\mathrm{DIM}^{2}(D^{A}_{s},Y^{A})}{\mathrm{VAR}(D^{A}_{s},Y^{A})}+\mathrm{N}_{A}^{i}(D^{A}_{s}),\quad\forall s\in[n_{mc}].

11:

Store the
n_{\mathrm{mc}}
Monte Carlo draws
\{D_{s}^{A}\}_{s=1}^{n_{\mathrm{mc}}}
as
\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{mc}}_{n_{A}}
.

12:

return
pval,
L
,
\mathcal{D}^{mc}_{n_{A}}

The following algorithm uses a bisection method to compute a lower bound for a given test statistic. The initial feasibility problem identifies a feasible set of outcomes whose test statistic exceeds this lower bound; by construction, this feasibility problem is always feasible. Recall that
i\in\{0,1,2\}
denotes the type of statistics that is used to test the balance of the number of always-reporters.

Algorithm 7

B_Create_Lower_Bound:
construct a lower bound for the test statistic (
17
), (
19
) and (
20
).

1:

assignment vector
D^{A}
, number of always-reporters
n_{A}
, dataset
\mathcal{D}
as in (
3
), lower bound
L
, upper bound
U
, tolerance
tol
.

2:

Calculate
N_{A}\leftarrow\mathrm{N}^{i}_{A}(D^{A})
.

3:

Solve the following feasibility problem: find
x=\left(x_{ai}\right)_{a,i}
and
Y^{A}=\left(Y_{a}\right)_{a}
that solve the problem,

4:

// If the bisection below does not update
L
, return compatiable outcomes.

\max_{x,Y^{A}}0

subject to,

Y_{a}=\sum_{i\in[n]}x_{ai}Y_{i},\quad\forall a\in[n_{A}],

Y_{a}\geq Y_{a+1},\quad\forall a\in[r_{0}+1,\,n_{A}-1],

\mathrm{DIM}^{2}(D^{A},Y^{A})+\bigl(N_{A}^{i}-L\bigr)\,\mathrm{VAR}(D^{A},Y^{A})\geq 0,

x\in x(n_{A},D^{\textnormal{obs}},R).

5:

Store the optimizer
Y^{A*}\leftarrow\left(Y_{a}^{*}\right)_{a}
.

6:

while

U-L\geq tol

do

7:

Set
M\leftarrow(L+U)/2
.

8:

Solve the following feasibility problem:

\max_{x,Y^{A}}0

9:

subject to,

Y_{a}=\sum_{i\in[n]}x_{ai}Y_{i},\quad\forall a\in[n_{A}],

Y_{a}\geq Y_{a+1},\quad\forall a\in[r_{0}+1,\,n_{A}-1],

\mathrm{DIM}^{2}(D^{A},Y^{A})+\bigl(N_{A}^{i}-M\bigr)\,\mathrm{VAR}(D^{A},Y^{A})\leq 0,

x\in x(n_{A},D^{\textnormal{obs}},R).

10:

if
the problem is feasible
then

11:

Set
U\leftarrow M
.
// The lower bound must be smaller than
U
.

12:

Store the optimizer
Y^{A*}\leftarrow\left(Y_{a}^{*}\right)_{a}
.

13:

else

14:

Set
L\leftarrow M
.
// The lower bound must be larger than
U
.

15:

end

if

16:

end

while

17:

return

L
,
\{Y_{a}^{*}\}_{a\in[n_{A}]}

Algorithm 8

B_Create_Lower_Bound_2:
construct a lower bound for statistics of the form
\mathrm{DIM}^{2}(D^{A},Y^{A})+c\mathrm{VAR}^{2}(D^{A},Y^{A})
given

and

and

, dataset

Solve the following feasibility problem: find
x=\left(x_{ai}\right)_{a,i}
,
Y^{A}=\left(Y_{a}\right)_{a}
and

that solves the problem:

// find the smallest

that’s compatible with an always-reporter table

\min_{x,Y^{A},t}t

subject to

Y_{a}=\sum_{i\in[n]}x_{ai}Y_{i},\quad\forall a\in[n_{A}],

x\in x(n_{A},D^{\textnormal{obs}},R),

\mathrm{DIM}^{2}(D^{A},Y^{A})+c\mathrm{VAR}(D^{A},Y^{A})\leq t,

\displaystyle t\geq L.

// only if
L
is provided.

Return the optimal value
t^{*}
;

The following algorithm computes a tighter upper bound for the test statistic, provided that the input
t_{\textrm{max}}
is a valid upper bound.

Algorithm 9

constructing an upper bound for the test statistic (

), (

) and (

.

Set
val\leftarrow-\infty
.

while

do

Solve the following feasibility problem: find
x=\left(x_{ai}\right)_{ai}
and

\max_{x,Y^{A}}0

subject to

Y_{a}=\sum_{i\in[n]}x_{ai}Y_{i},\quad\forall a\in[n_{A}],

Y_{a}\geq Y_{a+1},\quad\forall a\in[r_{0}+1,\,n_{A}-1],

\mathrm{DIM}^{2}(D^{A},Y^{A})+\bigl(\mathrm{N}^{i}_{A}-t_{\max}\bigr)\,\mathrm{VAR}(D^{a},Y^{a})\geq 0.

\min\{t_{\max},U\}

The algorithm below solves the subproblems described in Theorem
5.3
. We include a preprocessing step (Block 1) to compute tighter upper bounds for the simulated statistics. In practice, this step is useful because it yields a quick upper bound on the p-value.

Algorithm 10

B_Calculate_p_Value_Upper_Bound:
constructing an upper bound for the p-value for a given number of always-reporters
n_{A}
.

dataset
\mathcal{D}
, number of always-reporters
n_{A}
, generated simulated assignments
\mathcal{D}^{mc}_{n_{A}}
, lower bound for the observed test statistics
L_{n_{A}}

Block 1: Create tighter upper bounds for the simulated statistics

Create
UB=\texttt{zeros}(nsim,1)

// To hold upper bound values

for
s=1
to

n_{mc}

do

Set
D^{A}=D^{mc}_{n_{A}}(s)

// Extract the
i
th assignment vector

N_{A}\leftarrow\mathrm{N}^{i}_{A}(D^{A})

Set
\textrm{DIM2\_L},\textrm{DIM2\_U}\leftarrow\texttt{C\_dim2\_bounds}(D^{A},\mathcal{D})
.

Set
\textrm{NUM\_L}\leftarrow\texttt{C\_v1\_min}(D^{A},\mathcal{D})+\texttt{C\_v0\_min}(D^{A},\mathcal{D})
.

Set
\textrm{NUM\_U}\leftarrow\texttt{C\_v1\_max}(D^{A},\mathcal{D})+\texttt{C\_v0\_max}(D^{A},\mathcal{D})
.

Set
L\leftarrow\textrm{DIM2\_L}/\textrm{NUM\_U}+N_{A}
.

Set
U\leftarrow\textrm{DIM2\_U}/\textrm{NUM\_L}+N_{A}
.

UB(s)=\texttt{B\_Create\_Upper\_Bound}(D^{A},\mathcal{D},U)

end

for

Block 2: Preprocess based on the upper bounds

Calculate
pval=\texttt{mean}(UB>=L_{n_{A}})
.

if

pval<\alpha

then

// Many upper bounds of the simulated statistics are smaller than the lower bound of the observed statistic

return

pval

else

Calculate the
(1-\alpha)\times 100
percentile of
UB
,
t_{max}
.

Create a grid
t=L_{n_{A}}:0.01:t_{max}
. Index
t=\{t_{k}\}_{k=0}^{K}

for
k=1
to
K
do

Calculate
N_{A,s}=\mathrm{N}^{i}_{A}\left(D^{mc}_{n_{A}}(s)\right)
for all
s\in[n_{mc}]
.

Calculate
N_{A,\textnormal{obs}}=\mathrm{N}^{i}_{A}(D^{A}_{\textnormal{obs}})
.

Calculate
L_{s}=\texttt{B\_Create\_Lower\_Bound\_2}(\mathcal{D},D^{mc}_{n_{A}}(s),N_{A,s}-t_{i-1})
for all
s\in[n_{mc}]
.

Solves the optimization problem

\max\sum_{s=1}^{n_{mc}}I_{s},

subject to

Y_{a}=\sum_{i\in[n]}x_{ai}Y_{i},\quad\forall a\in[n_{A}],

Y_{a}\geq Y_{a+1},\quad\forall a\in[r_{0}+1,\,n_{A}-1],

// We write
D^{mc}_{n_{A}}(s)
as
D^{A}_{s}
below for simplicity.

\displaystyle\mathrm{DIM}^{2}(D^{A}_{s},Y^{A})+\Bigl(\mathrm{N}^{i}_{A,s}-t_{i-1}\Bigr)\mathrm{VAR}(D^{A}_{s},Y^{A})\ \geq\ L_{s}\,(1-I_{s}),\forall s\in[n_{mc}],

\displaystyle\mathrm{DIM}^{2}(D^{A}_{\textnormal{obs}},Y^{A})+\Bigl(\mathrm{N}^{i}_{A,\textnormal{obs}}-t_{i}\Bigr)\mathrm{VAR}(D^{A}_{\textnormal{obs}},Y^{A})\leq\ 0,

\displaystyle x\in x(k,D^{\textnormal{obs}},R),\qquad I_{s}\in\{0,1\},\qquad\forall s\in[n_{mc}].

Store
v(i)=\sum_{s=1}^{n_{mc}}I^{*}_{s}
.

end

for

return

\max_{i\in[K]}v(i)
.

end

if

Algorithm 12

B_Asymptotic_Inferences_Inner:

, number of always-reporters

, statistic type

, signifiancne level

.
