\mathcal{T}^{*}=\mathcal{T}^{\textnormal{obs}}\left(D^{A}_{\textnormal{obs}},Y^{A}\right)
, where,

\mathcal{T}^{\textnormal{obs}}(D^{A}_{\textnormal{obs}},Y^{A})=\frac{\mathrm{DIM}^{2}(D^{A}_{\textnormal{obs}},Y^{A})}{\mathrm{VAR}(D^{A}_{\textnormal{obs}},Y^{A})}+\mathrm{N}_{A}^{i}(D^{A}_{\textnormal{obs}}).

11:

Calculate the
1-\alpha
quantile,
q^{1-\alpha}
, of random variables
\textrm{Z}+g_{i}(n_{A}^{1})
, where
n_{A}^{1}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}A_{i}
,
\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}=n_{A}
and
D\sim\textrm{CR}(n,n_{1})
.

12:

if

\mathcal{T}^{*}>q^{1-\alpha}

then

13:

Set
Rej=1
.

14:

else

15:

Set
Rej=0
.

16:

end

if

17:

return

Rej
.

C.4
Analytical bounds for the studentized statistic

Given the dataset
\mathcal{D}=(Y,D^{\textnormal{obs}},R)
, denote the set of potential always-reporters in the observed treated group as
\mathbb{A}^{1}(D,R)=\{i\in[n]:D^{\textnormal{obs}}_{i}=1,R_{i}=1\}
. Denote the cardinality of this set as
n_{A,p}=\left|\mathbb{A}^{1}(D^{\textnormal{obs}},R)\right|
. Recall our convention that first
r_{0}
units are always-reporters assigned to the control group.

C.4.1
Lower Bound and Upper Bound for
\mathrm{DIM}^{2}
of (
139
)

Given outcome vector
Y=\left(Y_{i}\right)_{i=1}^{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and the set of potential always-reporters
\mathbb{A}^{1}(D^{\textnormal{obs}},R)
, we use
\widetilde{Y}_{(k)}
,
k\in[n_{A,p}]
to denote the
k
th smallest outcome in the set
\{Y_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{A}^{1}(D,R)}
, with
\widetilde{Y}_{(1)}
being the smallest.

For a fixed number of always-reporters, a standard argument gives the following upper bound and lower bound for the squared difference-in-means estimator
\mathrm{DIM}^{2}(\cdot,\cdot)
as defined in (
139
).

Lemma C.1
.

Given a positive integer
n_{A}
and the dataset
\mathcal{D}=(Y,D^{\textnormal{obs}},R)
, recall the set of assignment variables
x\left(n_{A},D^{\textnormal{obs}},R\right)
from (
41
). Denote the set of all compatible outcome vectors as

\displaystyle\mathbb{Y}(n_{A},D^{\textnormal{obs}},R,Y)

(147)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\left(Y_{a}\right)_{a\in[n_{A}]}:\exists\{x_{ai}\}_{a,i}\in x\left(n_{A},D^{\textnormal{obs}},R\right),Y_{a}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}x_{ai}Y_{i},\forall a\in[n_{A}]\right\}.

Given an assignment vector
D^{A}=\left(D_{a}\right)_{a=1}^{n_{A}}
, we have

\min_{Y^{A}\in\mathbb{Y}(n_{A},D^{\textnormal{obs}},R,Y)}\mathrm{DIM}^{2}(D^{A},Y^{A})\geq L(D^{A},\mathcal{D})

and,

\max_{Y^{A}\in\mathbb{Y}(n_{A},D^{\textnormal{obs}},R,Y)}\mathrm{DIM}^{2}(D^{A},Y^{A})\leq U(D^{A},\mathcal{D}).

where
L(D^{A},\mathcal{D})
and
U(D^{A},\mathcal{D})
are the lower bound and upper bound returned from
\texttt{C\_dim2\_bounds}(D^{A},\mathcal{D})
as defined in Algorithm
13
.

Algorithm 13

C_dim2_bounds
: Calculate a lower bound and an upper bound for the squared difference-in-means estimator

D^{A}=(D_{a})_{a\in[n_{A}]}
, Observed dataset
\mathcal{D}=\left(Y,D^{\textnormal{obs}},R\right)

Create
\mathbb{A}^{1}(D^{\textnormal{obs}},R)
and set
n_{A,p}=\left|\mathbb{A}^{1}(D^{\textnormal{obs}},R)\right|
.

Set
n_{A,1}=\sum_{a=1}^{n_{A}}D_{a}
,
r_{0,1}=\sum_{a=1}^{r_{0}}D_{a}
.

Set
n_{A,0}=\sum_{a=1}^{n_{A}}\left(1-D_{a}\right)
,
r_{0,0}=\sum_{a=1}^{r_{0}}\left(1-D_{a}\right)
.

Calculate
L
and
U
according to

\displaystyle U=\frac{1}{n_{A,1}}\left(\sum_{a=1}^{r_{0}}D_{a}Y_{a}+\sum_{k=1}^{n_{A,1}-r_{0,1}}\widetilde{Y}_{(n_{A,p}-k)}\right)-\frac{1}{n_{A,0}}\left(\sum_{a=1}^{r_{0}}\left(1-D_{a}\right)Y_{a}+\sum_{k=1}^{n_{A,0}-r_{0,0}}\widetilde{Y}_{(k)}\right),

and

L=\frac{1}{n_{A,1}}\left(\sum_{a=1}^{r_{0}}D_{a}Y_{a}+\sum_{k=1}^{n_{A,1}-r_{0,1}}\widetilde{Y}_{(k)}\right)-\frac{1}{n_{A,0}}\left(\sum_{a=1}^{r_{0}}\left(1-D_{a}\right)Y_{a}+\sum_{k=1}^{n_{A,0}-r_{0,0}}\widetilde{Y}_{(n_{A,p}-k)}\right).

if

L\leq U\leq 0
or
U\geq L\geq 0

then

return

\min\{L^{2},U^{2}\},\max\{L^{2},U^{2}\}

else

if

L\leq 0\leq U

then

return

0,\max\{L^{2},U^{2}\}

end

if

In words,
n_{A,1}
and
n_{A,0}
are the numbers of always-reporters assigned to the treated and control groups, respectively.
r_{0,1}
and
r_{0,0}
are the numbers of known always-reporters that are assigned to the treated and control groups with the assignment vector
\left(D_{a}\right)_{a=1}^{n_{A}}
. The upper bound and the lower bound follow from a standard calculation.

C.4.2
Lower Bound and Upper Bound for
\mathrm{VAR}
of (
140
)

For a fixed number of always-reporters and by Lemma
C.3
and Lemma
C.4
below, we have the following upper bound and lower bound for the variance estimator
\mathrm{VAR}(\cdot,\cdot)
as defined in (
140
). We note that
\mathrm{VAR}
is a standard two sample variance estimator and it can be written as the sum of the variance estimator for the treated group and the variance estimator for the control group.

Lemma C.2
.

Given a positive integer
n_{A}
and the dataset
\mathcal{D}=(Y,D^{\textnormal{obs}},R)
, recall the set of assignment variables
x\left(n_{A},D^{\textnormal{obs}},R\right)
from (
41
). Recall the set
\mathbb{Y}(n_{A},D^{\textnormal{obs}},R,Y)
of compatible outcome vectors
x\left(n_{A},D^{\textnormal{obs}},R\right)
as defined in (
147
).

Given an assignment vector
D^{A}=\left(D_{a}\right)_{a=1}^{n_{A}}
, we have

\min_{Y^{A}\in\mathbb{Y}(n_{A},D^{\textnormal{obs}},R,Y)}\mathrm{VAR}(D^{A},Y^{A})\geq v^{1}_{\min}+v^{0}_{\min},

and,

\max_{Y^{A}\in\mathbb{Y}(n_{A},D^{\textnormal{obs}},R,Y)}\mathrm{VAR}(D^{A},Y^{A})\leq v^{1}_{\max}+v^{0}_{\max}.

where
v^{1}_{\max}
,
v^{0}_{\max}
,
v^{1}_{\min}
and
v^{0}_{\min}
are the outputs of functions
\texttt{C\_v1\_max}(D^{A},\mathcal{D})
,
\texttt{C\_v0\_max}(D^{A},\mathcal{D})
,
\texttt{C\_v1\_min}(D^{A},\mathcal{D})
, and

\texttt{C\_v0\_min}(D^{A},\mathcal{D})
respectively.

Proof.

This is a direct corollary of Lemma
C.3
and Lemma
C.4
proved below.
∎

C.4.3
Proof of Lemma
C.2

Let
S=\{s_{1},...,s_{k_{1}}\}\subset\mathbb{R}
be a set of
k_{1}
points and
V=\{v_{1},...,v_{k_{2}}\}\subset\mathbb{R}
be a set of
k_{2}
points. For each
X=\{x_{1},...,x_{|X|}\}
such that
S\subset X\subset S\cup V
, we define the variance of
X
as

\texttt{var}\left(X\right)=\frac{1}{|X|}\sum_{i=1}^{|X|}\left(x_{i}-\overline{X}\right)^{2},\text{ with }\overline{X}=\frac{1}{|X|}\sum_{i=1}^{|X|}x_{i}.

(148)

We are interested in the maximum and minimum values of
\texttt{var}\left(X\right)
among the sets with the same cardinality which contain
S
. We call the sets
S
and
V

increasingly-ordered
if
s_{1}\leq s_{2}\leq...\leq s_{k_{1}}
and
v_{1}\leq v_{2}\leq...\leq v_{k_{2}}
.

Lemma C.3
.

Given increasingly-ordered sets
S=\{s_{i}\}_{i=1}^{k_{1}}\subset\mathbb{R}
,
V=\{v_{i}\}_{i=1}^{k_{2}}\subset\mathbb{R}
, and a positive integer
k_{1}+t
, with
t\geq 0
, define the set

\mathbb{X}^{t}=\{X:S\subset X\subset S\cup V,|X|=k_{1}+t\}.

Denote the set of consecutive subsets of
V
with size
t
as

\mathbb{V}^{t}_{c}=\left\{\{v_{i+1},..,v_{i+t}\}:i\in[0,k_{2}-t],i\in\mathbb{Z}\right\}.

Then we have:

\min_{X\in\mathbb{X}^{t}}\texttt{var}\left(X\right)=\min_{X=S\cup V_{c},V_{c}\in\mathbb{V}_{c}^{t}}\texttt{var}\left(X\right).

Proof.

Let
\mathbb{V}^{t}
be all subsets of
V
with size
t
.
Let
V_{nc}\in\mathbb{V}^{t}\backslash\mathbb{V}^{t}_{c}
be a non-consecutive set. List its element as
\{\widetilde{v}^{1},...,\widetilde{v}^{t}\}
in the order induced by
V
.

There are two cases.
If

\bigl(V\setminus V_{nc}\bigr)\cap[\widetilde{v}^{1},\,\widetilde{v}^{\,t}]\subseteq\{\widetilde{v}^{1},\,\widetilde{v}^{\,t}\}
,
then
V_{nc}
is non-consecutive only because of ties and the labeling order of observations. In this case, the variance of
V_{nc}
coincides with that of a consecutive subset.

For the other case, we have a
v\in\bigl(V\setminus V_{nc}\bigr)\cap[\widetilde{v}^{1},\,\widetilde{v}^{t}]
such that
v\in(\widetilde{v}^{1},\widetilde{v}^{t})
. We show that such a set cannot be a minimizing set, because we can always construct another set that has a strictly smaller variance than that of
V_{nc}
.

To see this, we note by definition
v=\lambda\widetilde{v}^{1}+(1-\lambda)\widetilde{v}^{t}
with
\lambda\in(0,1)
. Define
m=t+k_{1}
. The variance of the set
\left(S\cup V_{nc}\cup v\right)\backslash\widetilde{v}^{1}
can be expressed as:

\displaystyle\texttt{var}\left(\left(S\cup V_{nc}\cup v\right)\backslash\widetilde{v}^{1}\right)=\frac{1}{2m^{2}}\sum_{a,b\in S\cup V_{nc}\backslash\{\widetilde{v}^{1},\widetilde{v}^{t},v\}}(a-b)^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m^{2}}\left(\sum_{a\in S\cup V_{nc}\backslash\{\widetilde{v}^{1},\widetilde{v}^{t}\}}(v-a)^{2}+(v-\widetilde{v}^{t})^{2}+\sum_{a\in S\cup V_{nc}\backslash\widetilde{v}^{1}}(\widetilde{v}^{t}-a)^{2}\right),

and the variance of the set
\left(S\cup V_{nc}\cup v\right)\backslash\widetilde{v}^{t}
can be expressed as:

\displaystyle\texttt{var}\left(\left(S\cup V_{nc}\cup v\right)\backslash\widetilde{v}^{t}\right)=\frac{1}{2m^{2}}\sum_{a,b\in S\cup V_{nc}\backslash\{\widetilde{v}^{1},\widetilde{v}^{t},v\}}(a-b)^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m^{2}}\left(\sum_{a\in S\cup V_{nc}\backslash\{\widetilde{v}^{1},\widetilde{v}^{t}\}}(v-a)^{2}+(v-\widetilde{v}^{1})^{2}+\sum_{a\in S\cup V_{nc}\backslash\widetilde{v}^{t}}(\widetilde{v}^{1}-a)^{2}\right).

Then,

\displaystyle\lambda\texttt{var}\left(\left(S\cup V_{nc}\cup v\right)\backslash\widetilde{v}^{1}\right)+\left(1-\lambda\right)\texttt{var}\left(\left(S\cup V_{nc}\cup v\right)\backslash\widetilde{v}^{t}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2m^{2}}\sum_{a,b\in S\cup V_{nc}\backslash\{\widetilde{v}^{1},\widetilde{v}^{t},v\}}(a-b)^{2}+\frac{1}{m^{2}}\sum_{a\in S\cup V_{nc}\backslash\{\widetilde{v}^{1},\widetilde{v}^{t}\}}(v-a)^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{m^{2}}\left(\lambda(v-\widetilde{v}^{t})^{2}+\left(1-\lambda\right)(v-\widetilde{v}^{1})^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{\lambda}{m^{2}}\sum_{a\in S\cup V_{nc}\backslash\widetilde{v}^{1}}(\widetilde{v}^{t}-a)^{2}+\frac{1-\lambda}{m^{2}}\sum_{a\in S\cup V_{nc}\backslash\widetilde{v}^{t}}(\widetilde{v}^{1}-a)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2m^{2}}\sum_{a,b\in S\cup V_{nc}\backslash\{\widetilde{v}^{1},\widetilde{v}^{t},v\}}(a-b)^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{\lambda}{m^{2}}\sum_{a\in S\cup V_{nc}\backslash\{\widetilde{v}^{1},\widetilde{v}^{t}\}}\left(\widetilde{v}^{1}-a\right)^{2}+\frac{1-\lambda}{m^{2}}\sum_{a\in S\cup V_{nc}\backslash\{\widetilde{v}^{1},\widetilde{v}^{t}\}}\left(\widetilde{v}^{t}-a\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{m^{2}}\left(\lambda^{3}+(1-\lambda)^{3}\right)\left(\widetilde{v}^{1}-\widetilde{v}^{t}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{\lambda}{m^{2}}\sum_{a\in S\cup V_{nc}\backslash\widetilde{v}^{1}}(\widetilde{v}^{t}-a)^{2}+\frac{1-\lambda}{m^{2}}\sum_{a\in S\cup V_{nc}\backslash\widetilde{v}^{t}}(\widetilde{v}^{1}-a)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2m^{2}}\sum_{a,b\in S\cup V_{nc}\backslash\{\widetilde{v}^{1},\widetilde{v}^{t},v\}}(a-b)^{2}+\frac{1}{m^{2}}\left(\lambda^{3}+(1-\lambda)^{3}\right)\left(\widetilde{v}^{1}-\widetilde{v}^{t}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{m^{2}}\left(\sum_{a\in S\cup V_{nc}\backslash\widetilde{v}^{1}}(\widetilde{v}^{t}-a)^{2}+\sum_{a\in S\cup V_{nc}\backslash\widetilde{v}^{t}}(\widetilde{v}^{1}-a)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2m^{2}}\sum_{a,b\in S\cup V_{nc}\backslash\{\widetilde{v}^{1},\widetilde{v}^{t},v\}}(a-b)^{2}+\frac{1}{m^{2}}\left(\widetilde{v}^{1}-\widetilde{v}^{t}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{m^{2}}\left(\sum_{a\in S\cup V_{nc}\backslash\widetilde{v}^{1}}(\widetilde{v}^{t}-a)^{2}+\sum_{a\in S\cup V_{nc}\backslash\widetilde{v}^{t}}(\widetilde{v}^{1}-a)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\texttt{var}\left(S\cup V_{nc}\right),

where the first inequality is by convexity and the last inequality is by
\lambda^{3}+(1-\lambda^{3})<1
for
\lambda\in(0,1)
because
1=(\lambda+1-\lambda)^{3}=\lambda^{3}+\left(1-\lambda\right)^{3}+3\lambda(1-\lambda)^{2}+3(1-\lambda)^{2}
.
Hence either
\texttt{var}\left(S\cup V_{nc}\right)>\texttt{var}\left(\left(S\cup V_{nc}\cup v\right)\backslash\widetilde{v}^{1}\right)
or
\texttt{var}\left(S\cup V_{nc}\right)>\texttt{var}\left(\left(S\cup V_{nc}\cup v\right)\backslash\widetilde{v}^{t}\right)
, and
S\cup V_{nc}
cannot be the optimizing set.

We have shown that for a non-consecutive set, it either realizes a variance equal to that of a consecutive set or cannot be the optimizing set. This proves our claim.

∎

Lemma C.4
.

Given increasingly-ordered sets
S=\{s_{i}\}_{i=1}^{k_{1}}\subset\mathbb{R}
,
V=\{v_{i}\}_{i=1}^{k_{2}}\subset\mathbb{R}
, and a positive integer
k_{1}+t
, with
0\leq t\leq k_{2}
, define the set

\mathbb{X}^{t}=\{X:S\subset X\subset S\cup V,|X|=k_{1}+t\}.

Denote the set of shell sets of
V
with size
t
as

\mathbb{V}^{t}_{s}=\{\{v_{1},...,v_{s}\}\cup\{v_{k_{2}-(t-s-1)},...,v_{k_{2}}\}:s\in[0,t]\},

where
\{v_{1},...,v_{s}\}=\emptyset
if
s=0
and
\{v_{k_{2}-(t-s-1)},...,v_{k_{2}}\}=\emptyset
if
s=t
.
Then we have:

\max_{X\in\mathbb{X}^{t}}\texttt{var}\left(X\right)=\max_{X=S\cup V_{s},V_{s}\in\mathbb{V}_{s}^{t}}\texttt{var}\left(X\right).

Proof.

For the case where
t=k_{2}
, the statement is trivial. We consider the case where
t<k_{2}
. Let
\mathbb{V}^{t}
be all subsets of
V
with size
t
.
For each
\widetilde{V}\in\mathbb{V}^{t}
, we define its left-shell size and right-shell size as:

\textrm{Left-Shell-Size}\left(\widetilde{V}\right)=\max\left\{k:\{v_{1},v_{2},...,v_{k}\}\subset\widetilde{V}\right\},

(149)

\textrm{Right-Shell-Size}\left(\widetilde{V}\right)=\max\left\{k:\{v_{k_{2}-k+1},...v_{k_{2}-1},v_{k_{2}}\}\subset\widetilde{V}\right\}.

(150)

We note that
\widetilde{V}\in\mathbb{V}^{t}
is a shell set if and only if

\textrm{Left-Shell-Size}\left(\widetilde{V}\right)+\textrm{Right-Shell-Size}\left(\widetilde{V}\right)=t.

(151)

We show that we can always transform a non-shell set to a shell set through consecutive steps and weakly increase the variance.

Suppose the set
V_{ns}
that is not a shell set.
Then there must exists a
v_{s}\in V_{ns}
, and
v_{s_{l}},v_{s_{r}}\not\in V_{ns}
such that
s_{l}<s<s_{r}
.

Define

l=\min\{i\in[k_{2}]:v_{i}\leq v,v_{i}\not\in V_{ns}\},r=\max\{i\in[k_{2}]:v_{i}\geq v,v_{i}\not\in V_{ns}\}.

Moreover,
v_{s}=\lambda v_{l}+(1-\lambda)v_{r}
for some
\lambda\in[0,1]
. Let
m=k+t_{1}
.
The variance of the set
V_{ns}\cup v_{l}\backslash v_{s}
can be expressed as

\displaystyle\texttt{var}\left(V_{ns}\cup v_{l}\backslash v_{s}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2m^{2}}\sum_{a,b\in V_{ns}\backslash v_{s}}(a-b)^{2}+\frac{1}{m^{2}}\sum_{a\in V_{ns}\backslash v_{s}}(v_{l}-a)^{2},

and the variance of the set
V_{ns}\cup v_{r}\backslash v_{s}
can be expressed as

\displaystyle\texttt{var}\left(V_{ns}\cup v_{r}\backslash v_{s}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2m^{2}}\sum_{a,b\in V_{ns}\backslash v_{s}}(a-b)^{2}+\frac{1}{m^{2}}\sum_{a\in V_{ns}\backslash v_{s}}(v_{r}-a)^{2},

By convexity, we have

\texttt{var}\left(V_{ns}\right)\leq\lambda\texttt{var}\left(V_{ns}\cup v_{l}\backslash v_{s}\right)+(1-\lambda)\texttt{var}\left(V_{ns}\cup v_{r}\backslash v_{s}\right).

WLOG, if
\texttt{var}\left(V_{ns}\right)<\texttt{var}\left(V_{ns}\backslash v\cup v_{r}\right)
, replacing
v
with
v_{r}
will strictly increase the variance and increase the shell size of the new set. If
\texttt{var}\left(V_{ns}\right)=\texttt{var}\left(V_{ns}\cup v_{r}\backslash v_{s}\right)=\texttt{var}\left(V_{ns}\cup v_{l}\backslash v_{s}\right)
, replacing
v_{s}
with either
v_{r}
and
v_{l}
will not decrease the variance and increase the shell size of the new set.

Repeat the same procedure multiple times. Since there are at most
t
points in the set, the procedure will stop after at most
t
steps and output a shell set. Since each step we do not decrease the variance, the new shell set has a variance at least as large as the that of the set
V_{ns}
. This proves the claim.

∎

C.4.4
Algorithms according to Lemma
C.3
and Lemma
C.4

Given the observed dataset
\mathcal{D}=\left(Y,D^{\textnormal{obs}},R\right)
, we denote the set of potential always-reporters in the observed treated group as
\mathbb{A}^{1}(D,R)=\{i\in[n]:D^{\textnormal{obs}}_{i}=1,R_{i}=1\}
and denote
n_{A,p}=\left|\mathbb{A}^{1}(D,R)\right|
.

Recall the notation that we use
\widetilde{Y}_{(k)}
,
k\in[n_{A,p}]
to denote the
k
th smallest outcome in the set
\{Y_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{A}^{1}(D,R)}
, with
\widetilde{Y}_{(1)}
being the smallest. We note that
D_{a}=D_{i}
for
a,i\leq r_{0}
by our by indexing convention.

Algorithm 14

C_v1_min
: Calculate a lower bound for the treated group variance estimator

D^{A}=(D_{a})_{a\in[n_{A}]}
, Observed dataset
\mathcal{D}=\left(Y,D^{\textnormal{obs}},R\right)

\mathbb{A}^{1}(D^{\textnormal{obs}},R)
and set
n_{A,p}=\left|\mathbb{A}^{1}(D^{\textnormal{obs}},R)\right|

Set

,
r_{0,1}=\sum_{a=1}^{r_{0}}D_{a}
,
n_{A,p}=\left|\mathbb{A}^{1}(D^{\textnormal{obs}},R)\right|
.

for

do

.

if

v_{k}\leq v_{\min}^{1}

then

v_{\min}^{1}\leftarrow v_{k}

end

if

end

forreturn

v_{\min}^{1}

.

.

and set

,

,

.

We note that
\{\widetilde{Y}_{(s)}\}_{s=1}^{s=k}=\emptyset
if
k=0
and

if
k=n_{A,1}-r_{0,1}
.

Algorithm 17

C_v0_max
: Calculate an upper bound for the control group variance estimator

D^{A}=(D_{a})_{a\in[n_{A}]}
, Observed dataset
\mathcal{D}=\left(Y,D^{\textnormal{obs}},R\right)

Create
\mathbb{A}^{1}(D^{\textnormal{obs}},R)
and set
n_{A,p}=\left|\mathbb{A}^{1}(D^{\textnormal{obs}},R)\right|

Set
n_{A,0}=\sum_{a=1}^{n_{A}}\left(1-D_{a}\right)
,
r_{0,0}=\sum_{a=1}^{r_{0}}\left(1-D_{a}\right)
.

v^{0}_{\max}\leftarrow-\infty

for

k=0:\left(n_{A,0}-r_{0,0}\right)

do

Create set
X_{k}=\{Y_{i}\}_{i\leq r_{0},D_{i}=0}\bigcup\{\widetilde{Y}_{(s)}\}_{s=1}^{s=k}\bigcup\{\widetilde{Y}_{(s)}\}_{s=n_{A,p}-n_{A,0}+r_{0,0}+k+1}^{s=n_{A,p}}

Calculate Calculate
v_{k}=\texttt{var}(X_{k})
.

if

v_{k}\geq v^{0}_{\max}

then

v^{0}_{\max}\leftarrow v_{k}

end

if

end

forreturn

v^{0}_{\max}
